**Verified three separate ways, not just reasoned through:** (1) An *isolated* comparison — same known cover URL, rendered via `object-fit: cover` in one box vs. the natural-scale-filmstrip technique in an identically-sized sibling box, at all four carousel width tiers (real card widths measured live: ~240/252/272/332px) and two height scenarios each. Pixel-diffed with `sharp`: near-zero at the shorter height, a small (~2–3% average byte delta) but visually-imperceptible difference at the taller height, consistent with ordinary sub-pixel rasterization rounding between the two techniques (the same class of artifact this file's own filmstrip mirror-seam fix, `marginTop: -3`, already exists to paper over elsewhere) — not a different crop. Confirmed by eye, side by side: indistinguishable. (2) A *live app* pixel-diff was tried first and discarded as invalid: `scoring.ts` picks results with `Math.random()`, so two separate page loads with the identical tag selection can still surface entirely different covers — a 90%+ byte delta there was two different anime, not a regression. (3) Measured the filmstrip's own real, natural rendered height against real covers at the widest carousel tier (332px cards) — ~460–510px per copy, ~500px+ of margin over the card's own ~380–445px real height range — confirming "room to spare" isn't a narrow margin anywhere in this app's actual size range, carousel or grid mode alike (grid mode's own narrower ~217px cards checked too: the mirror does visibly kick in there, correctly, roughly the bottom half of the tag area on a tall viewport).

**Reused, not reinvented:** the continuation scrim (from round one) is now unconditional rather than gated — the seam always exists, at every size, so there's no case left where it would double up with anything else. The veil (`--cover-reflection-veil`) needed no changes at all; it was never gated by height even in round one.

**The generalized tell, refined from round one's own version of it: when several boxes need to look like one continuous image but must stay independently sized for layout reasons, don't crop each one separately and align the crops with math (a hardcoded offset like the old `COVER_HEIGHT_PX`, valid at exactly one size) — give one shared, unclipped, natural-scale layer the combined space instead, and let each box's own existing clipping reveal its own slice of it.** The boxes never need to agree on a coordinate system, and the transition between "real content" and "whatever comes after" (here, a mirror; conceivably anything else stacked the same way) falls out of ordinary document flow instead of being computed against anything external.

**Round three: the filmstrip's own mirror image regressed `TagScrollArea`'s touch-drag scroll, real-iOS-only — and the first fix attempt (`touch-none` on the filmstrip) was wrong, confirmed live.** Reported live, only on real Safari/Chrome-iOS (both — same underlying WebKit) — doesn't reproduce in headless or emulated Chromium at all. A plain `elementFromPoint()` at the touch coordinate already resolves correctly to `TagScrollArea`'s own scroll viewport, so this was never a DOM hit-testing bug the way the small-card continuation's own reveal-through mechanism was.

First attempt: added `pointer-events-none`/`touch-none` to the filmstrip wrapper, reasoning that the mirror image's `transform: scaleY(-1)` (needed for the flip, no non-transform way to do it in CSS) promotes it onto its own GPU compositing layer, and that a composited sibling overlapping a scroll container's region might confuse WebKit's own touch routing independent of `pointer-events`. **Confirmed live: did not fix it.**

The actual mechanism — and the fix — was already directly established earlier in this app, for a *different* scroll container: `RecommendationsBody`'s own carousel viewport (`scrollRef`, its horizontal `ScrollFadeArea`). That fix's own comment (`RecommendationsBody.tsx`) states the precise rule this round had lost sight of: **`pointer-events: none` disqualifies a scroll container from native touch-scroll-gesture-*chaining* entirely on real mobile WebKit, not just from click hit-testing — but only when it's on the scroll container's *own* structural elements**, not an unrelated sibling. `TagScrollArea`'s own viewport was never `pointer-events: none` here (confirmed `auto` throughout) — so disqualifying-property theories (both the original `touch-none` attempt and the closely-related "GPU layer" theory) were reasoning about the wrong element. The original carousel fix's actual resolution was **explicit z-index (paint order) instead of a disqualifying property** — Clear ✕ wins its own hit-test via `z-[var(--z-raised)]`, so the carousel's own `scrollRef`/`root`/`contentRef` could go back to plain `pointer-events: auto` and keep receiving the gesture.

Applied the same resolution here, not the same property: removed `pointer-events-none`/`touch-none` from the filmstrip entirely (never actually needed for hit-testing correctness — cover-box's own opaque `bg-[var(--fill)]` and tag-wrapper's own box already win there by DOM order regardless of the filmstrip's own pointer-events), and gave **tag-wrapper an explicit `z-[var(--z-raised)]`** (was implicit `z-index: auto` + later-DOM-order-wins) — explicit z-index creates tag-wrapper's own stacking context, isolating `TagScrollArea`'s gesture handling from whatever compositing the filmstrip's transform needs, rather than leaving both to share `FRONT`'s stacking context together. Verified structurally (computed `pointer-events: auto`/`touch-action: auto` on the filmstrip, `z-index: 0`/`1` respectively on filmstrip/tag-wrapper, hit-test still resolves correctly, lock-button toggle and tap-to-flip both still work) — the actual on-device effect still can't be confirmed outside a real iOS device, so this remains the leading hypothesis, just now backed by this app's own *confirmed*, not speculative, precedent.

**Audited for the same shape of risk elsewhere, per explicit request — found nothing else live:** `ScrollFadeArea`'s `disableWrapperPointerEvents` prop (the mechanism behind the original carousel bug) has no current callers at all — dormant, not a risk. `RecommendationsBody`'s own grid wrapper still carries `pointer-events-none` deliberately (a *different* wrapper than `scrollRef`, already reasoned through and documented there). The continuation scrim inside tag-wrapper (round one, this file) is also `pointer-events-none` and geometrically overlaps `TagScrollArea` — but it predates this regression (existed before the filmstrip/mirror), and is already safe on the same explicit-z-index logic: its own `z-[var(--z-raised)]` (1) loses to `TagScrollArea`'s wrapper's own `z-[2]`, so pointer-events-none there is redundant-but-harmless, not load-bearing. `MixerBar.tsx` has no `transform` usage at all. The one genuinely new risk this session introduced was the filmstrip's own always-mounted mirror transform — the thing just fixed.

**Round four: round three's own z-index addition regressed the *outer carousel* — a horizontal swipe stopped chaining through a card's tag area specifically once that tag area had real vertical overflow (few tags, no overflow: swipe always worked fine).** Reported live, real-iPhone-only, immediately after round three shipped. First checked whether the axis-split config itself regressed: it hadn't — `TagScrollArea`'s viewport still computed exactly `touch-action: pan-y` / `overscroll-behavior-x: auto` / `overscroll-behavior-y: contain`, the already-correct, already-precedented split (see `ScrollFadeArea.tsx`'s own comment on the identical carousel-vs-tag-scroll axis fix). Config being already correct, and the user confirming this exact scenario (swipe through an *active*, overflowing tag area) used to work before round three, pointed at round three's own change instead of a fresh CSS gap.

Two real culprits found, both introduced by round three, both removed:

1. **Tag-wrapper's own `z-[var(--z-raised)]`.** Round three's own comment already conceded this z-index was never fixing a hit-test gap — "this box already correctly won hit-testing that way (DOM order alone was never the actual gap)" — it was added speculatively, to isolate `TagScrollArea`'s stacking from the filmstrip mirror's compositing. That speculative isolation is what broke gesture-chaining: giving tag-wrapper its own stacking context sits it, as a compositing boundary, directly on the path between the carousel's own scroll viewport and `TagScrollArea`'s — a real iOS WebKit trap, once that inner path is an *active* (overflowing) scroll target and not just a pass-through. Reverted to plain `relative`, no explicit z-index. Re-verified (Playwright screenshot, front/back/front-again) that the local stacking order inside tag-wrapper (veil under continuation-scrim under the tag chips) still renders identically without it: the token values already in use (`z-base` = 0 for both the filmstrip and the veil, `z-raised` = 1 for the scrim, a literal `2` for the chips) resolve to the same visual order once "promoted" into `FRONT`'s own stacking context, because the tied-z-index pair (filmstrip vs. veil, both 0) still breaks by DOM order in the same direction that was already wanted.

2. **The flip-container's own `rotateY(0deg)` at rest — the identical trap as the earlier perspective-off-`<article>` fix (this file, "This alone got desktop... fully native," above), just missed on a second element.** That earlier fix moved `perspective` off the carousel's actual snap-item (`<article>`) onto a new wrapper specifically because a non-`none` transform-family property on the box the carousel's own gesture/snap math operates on confuses native scroll routing. The flip-container (one level further in, direct ancestor of `FRONT`/tag-wrapper/`TagScrollArea`) still carried an explicit, unconditional `transform: rotateY(0deg)` even at rest — visually the identity transform, but not `none`: still a live containing-block/compositing boundary sitting on the exact path between the carousel's scroll viewport and the tag-scroll's own, for as long as the card exists. Fixed the same way, not by rebuilding the mechanism: the `flipped ? "...rotateY(180deg)" : "...rotateY(0deg)"` pair became `flipped ? "...rotateY(180deg)" : ""` — omitting the class at rest lets `transform` fall back to its real initial value, confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` (`transform: "none"`, not a `matrix()` identity). Safe because: `none` and `rotateY(0deg)` are visually identical; `transition-transform` still interpolates smoothly between `none` and a real transform value in both directions (spec-supported, confirmed live across a full flip and flip-back with no visual glitch); and `FRONT`/`BACK`'s own `absolute inset-0` resolve to the same box either way, since the perspective wrapper one level up is already `position: relative` at this element's exact size — an equally valid absolute-positioning containing block whether or not the flip-container currently has its own. The transform still applies for the duration of an actual flip — that's required for the visual effect, and a card mid-flip isn't the moment a horizontal carousel swipe through its tag area needs to work anyway.

**Both fixes independently verified structurally (computed styles, Playwright screenshots of front/back/front-again with no visual difference) — the actual on-device gesture-chaining effect still needs real-iPhone confirmation, same as every other fix in this whole sequence.** The standing lesson this round adds: a stacking-context-isolation fix (z-index) and a 3D-transform fix are members of the *same* family of trap — anything that gives an element its own compositing/containing-block boundary, however that boundary is created, is a risk if that element sits between two scroll containers that need to chain a gesture through it. Confirm nothing sits on that path *unconditionally* before reaching for one of these properties as a fix for something else.

**Round five: rounds three and four were both real, defensible cleanups, but neither was the actual regression — a live, on-device git bisect found the real one.** After round four still didn't fix the reported symptom on a real iPhone, theorizing stopped in favor of bisecting: force-pushing successive candidate commits to a throwaway branch so each one gets a real Vercel preview URL, testing the exact scenario (horizontal carousel swipe starting over a card's tag area, *while that tag area has genuine vertical overflow*) on-device at each step. `fd0054d^` (right before any of the filmstrip work) was still broken. `cbcbc05` (right before the RatingBadge/`averageScore` feature, several commits earlier) **worked** — pinning the regression to somewhere in that range, unrelated to anything the filmstrip/cover-through-tag-area work ever touched.

Diffing `cbcbc05` against current `main` for exactly the files that could matter (`ResultCard.tsx`, `TagScrollArea.tsx`, `ScrollFadeArea.tsx`) surfaced the real change: `5dacb25` ("empty-slot closes edit mode + touch-action scroll-through at root," several commits before the filmstrip work even started) added `touch-pan-x`/`touch-pan-y` to `ScrollFadeArea`'s own scroll viewport — **unconditionally, for every caller.** Before that commit, every `ScrollFadeArea` instance ran on plain, unrestricted `touch-action: auto`; `overscroll-x/y-contain`'s own axis-scoping already existed and was untouched by it.

That commit had a real, narrow reason: MixerBar's own root carries `touch-none` (blocks every native pan by default), and exactly three `ScrollFadeArea` instances live inside it — MixerBar's own filters row, `SectionTabStrip`'s tab strip, `TagPoolBody`'s chip area — each needing to carve its own single axis back out from that ancestor `touch-none`, or they couldn't scroll at all. But the fix was applied inside the *shared* component, unconditionally, so it also landed on the two callers that have no such ancestor and never needed carving out anything: the results carousel (`RecommendationsBody`) and `TagScrollArea` (`ResultCard`'s per-card tag area). For those two, restricting to a single axis is actively counterproductive: `touch-action: auto` lets the browser's own default scroll-chaining hand an unsupported axis to the nearest ancestor that can actually move on it (this element tries to pan on whatever axis the touch turns out to be, finds no scrollable range on X, and the motion naturally passes up to the carousel) — `touch-action: pan-y` instead makes this element *not even attempt* to recognize a horizontal gesture as belonging to it at all, and on real iOS WebKit that "hand it fully to whichever ancestor supports it instead" step doesn't reliably happen, at least not once this element is a genuine, overflowing scroll candidate rather than empty pass-through space. Both explanations are consistent with everything reported across rounds three and four: few tags (no overflow) always worked, because there was no competing candidate to fail to hand off from.

**Fix: `restrictTouchAction`, a new opt-in prop on `ScrollFadeArea`** (default `false`), gating the `touch-pan-x`/`touch-pan-y` class instead of applying it unconditionally. Only MixerBar's three own callers pass it — `MixerBar.tsx`'s filters row, `SectionTabStrip.tsx`, `TagPoolBody.tsx`'s chip area, confirmed to be the *only* three `ScrollFadeArea` instances anywhere inside MixerBar's `touch-none` root. The results carousel and `TagScrollArea` don't pass it, so they fall back to their own pre-`5dacb25` behavior. Verified live (Playwright, computed `touchAction`): MixerBar's own three instances still read `pan-x`/`pan-y` exactly as before, unchanged; the carousel and card tag-scroll now both read plain `auto`, matching the confirmed-working `cbcbc05` state exactly. Rounds three and four's own fixes (tag-wrapper's z-index, the flip-container's `rotateY(0deg)`-at-rest) are kept regardless — both are real, harmless-or-beneficial cleanups (verified with zero visual regression), just not the cause of *this* bug.

**The shared-component-default lesson this round taught is generalized in its own entry now — see `ScrollFadeArea`'s own section, "A trap distinct from the compositing-boundary family above."**

**The methodology lesson is worth stating on its own too: when a theory about the mechanism keeps failing on-device, stop refining the theory and bisect instead.** Rounds three and four each built a real, defensible fix (paint-order z-index, then a 3D-transform-at-rest omission) for a plausible-sounding mechanism — a compositing/containing-block boundary sitting on the path between two scroll containers — and each one failed to fix the actual reported symptom on a real phone. Both were reasoning carefully about a real category of WebKit trap; neither was reasoning about *this* bug, because the actual cause (an unconditional class change several commits *earlier*, in a completely different feature) was never in the hypothesis space at all. No amount of additional theorizing about compositing was ever going to reach it.

What actually cracked it was a single piece of recalled information: *"it worked before we introduced the score/rating metric."* That's not a mechanism, it's a timestamp — and a live, on-device bisect (checking out successive candidate commits, pushing each to a throwaway branch for a real preview URL since a local dev server can't be tested reliably on a physical phone, testing the exact reported scenario at each step) turned that timestamp into an exact commit in four tests: broken at `fd0054d^`, working at `cbcbc05`, which narrowed the search to a small, diffable range and surfaced the real change (`5dacb25`'s unconditional `touch-pan-x`/`touch-pan-y`) directly, with no further guessing required. **The general rule: a memory of *when* something last worked is a strictly stronger diagnostic signal than a theory about *why* it's broken now, especially once more than one theory has already failed — it converts open-ended reasoning about an unbounded mechanism-space into a bounded, mechanical search over a known commit range.** Reach for it as soon as a first theory-driven fix fails to confirm on-device, not after a second or third.

### Round six: which of cover-box/tag-wrapper actually shrinks, reversed on purpose, with a floor so the tag area never goes negative

Every round above assumed cover-box was the one that shrinks (`flex-1`, sized off whatever `--result-card-height`'s own clamp left over) and tag-wrapper was `shrink-0`, protected at its own natural size (padding plus `TagScrollArea`'s then-flat 121px). Explicitly reversed by request: keep the cover (art, title, rating) legible at nearly every card size, and let the tag area give up room instead, accepting that a squeezed card might only show one chip row before it has to scroll.

**A naive swap (just flip `flex-1`/`shrink-0` between the two boxes) breaks two different ways, both found before writing any code.** First: cover-box has no intrinsic height of its own to fall back to (every child inside it, the scrim, the icon row, the title/meta stack, `RatingBadge`, is `absolute`), so simply marking it `shrink-0` with no explicit height collapses it to nothing. Second, and less obvious: rigidly fixing cover-box at its own full natural height (measured live at `--result-card-height`'s 445px ceiling: 303px) leaves *negative* room for tag-wrapper at `--result-card-height`'s own 322px floor (322 − 303 − border overhead comes out under tag-wrapper's own 16px of padding alone): the documented 375×667 small-phone case this file already tracks elsewhere would have shown literally zero tag content, not just "fewer tags."

**The fix: `--result-card-tag-area-height` gets its own floor, and cover only gives up room once that floor is hit.** Three new tokens in `globals.css`, next to `--result-card-height`:

- **`--result-card-tag-area-height`**: `clamp(63px, calc(var(--result-card-height) - 308px), 137px)`. The ceiling (137px) is tag-wrapper's old flat natural size, unchanged. The floor (63px) is hand-derived from `TagScrollArea.tsx`'s own constants, not shared automatically: `TOP_RESERVE_PX`(14) + one `ROW_STEP_PX`(33) = 47px of actual scrollable content, plus this box's own `space-md*2` (16px) padding, enough for one full chip row plus its usual top peek, never less.
- **`--result-card-cover-height`**: `calc(var(--result-card-height) - var(--result-card-tag-area-height) - (var(--border-base) * 2))`, whatever's left once the tag area and the card's own top+bottom border are accounted for.
- **`--result-card-tag-scroll-height`**: `--result-card-tag-area-height` minus this box's own padding, the value actually fed to `TagScrollArea.tsx`'s own `ScrollFadeArea`, replacing its old flat `h-[121px]`.

**The algebra is what makes cover stay genuinely fixed across most of the range, not just approximately stable.** Substituting the tag area's own ideal (unclamped) term into the cover formula: `result-card-height − (result-card-height − 308) − border-overhead` cancels the `result-card-height` dependency entirely, leaving a flat constant. Cover-box's height is therefore *exactly* unchanging for every `--result-card-height` from 445px down to where the tag area's own floor engages (~372px, live-measured), and only below that does cover-box start shrinking too, since the tag area can no longer absorb the difference on its own. Confirmed with a live sweep across ten viewport heights: `coverHeight` reads a flat `303` at every measured card height from 381px up to 445px, then steps down smoothly (`263` at 331px, `254` at 322px) only in that narrow bottom band, while `tagHeight` does the opposite, `138` down to `74` across the flat-cover range, then pinned at its own `64`px floor below that.

**A second, less obvious risk: a fixed-height child inside a shrunk parent doesn't just show fewer rows, it clips the fade gradient and down-arrow off entirely.** `ScrollFadeArea`'s bottom fade gradient and down-arrow button are both absolutely positioned against its own root, which sizes off its scroll-viewport child's height. If that child had stayed a flat `h-[121px]` while the outer tag-wrapper shrank around it, both would still be anchored to the bottom of the full, invisible 121px box, past the point the shrunk wrapper's own `overflow-hidden` actually clips, making them vanish instead of gracefully repositioning. This is why `--result-card-tag-scroll-height` exists as its own token rather than leaving `TagScrollArea` on its old constant: `TAG_AREA_HEIGHT` now reads `h-[var(--result-card-tag-scroll-height)]`, the same static-arbitrary-value-class-referencing-a-custom-property pattern `--result-card-height` itself already established (see "Runtime-computed dimensions" above), so `ScrollFadeArea`'s fade/arrow reposition to wherever the real, current bottom edge is. Verified live, not assumed: forced a card into an overflowing state at the 322px floor and read the fade gradient's and down-arrow's own `getBoundingClientRect()` directly, both fully inside tag-wrapper's shrunk 64px bounds (9px inset from its bottom edge), not sitting past it.

**Accepted, not fixed: two small rough edges concentrated at the very bottom of the range, where the tag area's floor is already engaged.** The specific 8px/22px peek amounts `TagScrollArea.tsx` documents for its own `TAG_AREA_HEIGHT` were tuned against one fixed 121px; they stop being pixel-exact once that height varies, though the underlying scroll/snap mechanics and the one-row floor keep working at every size in between. Separately, `scrollStep` (`VISIBLE_ROWS * ROW_STEP_PX`, 99px) doesn't shrink along with the visible area, so a single arrow click can jump further than one screen's worth of content once only one row is actually visible at once. Both are minor, isolated to the smallest card heights, and left alone rather than adding more machinery to chase pixel-exactness the original request didn't ask for.

**The floor was later raised from one row to two, by explicit request, once live testing confirmed genuinely small-phone heights were routinely landing right at the original one-row floor, not just some rare edge case.** `--result-card-tag-area-height`'s floor moved from `63px` to `96px` (`TOP_RESERVE_PX(14) + TWO ROW_STEP_PX(33 each) = 80px` of scrollable content, plus this box's own 16px padding), guaranteeing two full chip rows plus the usual top peek of a third, reading as roughly "2.5 rows," the same derivation shape as the original just-one-row floor, only with `N=2` instead of `N=1` in `TOP_RESERVE_PX + N*ROW_STEP_PX + 16px`. A direct, accepted consequence: the algebra above means the cover no longer stays flat for quite as long, since the tag area's own floor now engages at a taller `--result-card-height` (~404px, up from ~372px). The cover has to start giving up room a little sooner to make space for the second protected row, not a separate regression. Confirmed live at both realistic phone heights (375×667, 390×844): the actual scrollable viewport measures exactly 80px at each, matching the new floor precisely, and the ceiling (121px, ~3 rows, desktop) is untouched.

**Requested at the same time: apply whatever this floor becomes to `BACK`'s own two zones too, so the description/streaming sections keep the identical ratio when a card flips.** No separate change was needed for this part. `BACK`'s zone 2 (description/streaming) already reuses `--result-card-tag-area-height` directly (see "The back face's two zones now reuse FRONT's own cover/tag-area height tokens directly" below), so raising the one shared token moved both faces together automatically. Confirmed live: `BACK`'s own zone 2 measures the identical 80px at the same viewport heights `FRONT`'s tag area does.

## MixerBar — a sticky-bottom bar with a browse mode and an edit mode

`MixerSection` (a static, in-flow section) is now `MixerBar.tsx`, the last child of the page's own content column, always rendered. This supersedes the old Recommendations/Tag Pool tab toggle entirely (see "Tag Pool and Recommendations became two tabs… since retired" above): results are now the app's one persistent main surface, and the tag pool is *summoned* into this bar's edit mode rather than living as a peer tab.

**Three positioning eras, not two: `position: fixed` (Step 1), un-pinned to plain normal flow (Step 2), now `position: sticky; bottom-[space-2xl]` (Step 3)** — see "The single box that grows: how edit mode actually expands" below for the growth mechanism (unchanged across all three eras) and "`position: sticky` — floats when the page scrolls, rests in flow otherwise" below for what Step 3 changed and confirmed. This section documents the current shape only; earlier eras are intentionally not re-explained beyond what's needed to say why each was replaced.

### Browse mode and edit mode are one bar that grows, not two overlays

`KonomiApp` owns a single `editMode: boolean` (replacing the old `activeTab`, in the same place, for the same reason: `handleRecommend` needs to force it closed even when it's currently open, so RECOMMEND always lands the user back on the results it just produced, success or error). `MixerBar` renders two rows in a flex column (originally three, see below), and edit mode only changes what's *before* the first of them, it never introduces a second, separate overlay:

1. **Pool panel**: `TagPoolBody` (its own tab row — section tabs plus MixerBar's resize grip/shortcuts/close button, see below — then the chip area), present only in edit mode. Not a separate sheet, see "State preservation" below for why it's still always mounted regardless. Sits directly above the combined row (2, below), not inside it, where an earlier version placed it (see below for why).
2. **Combined slots + controls row**: originally two separate rows (a slots row, then a controls row), later combined into one, requested directly: the 3 `TagSlot`s, `TagSlot` itself dropped to a single, fixed, button-matching height for this to even be possible, grouped with Tag Pool/Clear on the left and Min Match/Random/a divider/RECOMMEND on the right. Everything below about this row's breakpoints and thresholds refers to it. See "Row 2 (combined)" further below for the full merge writeup.

**No header row in either mode.** An earlier version had a fourth row above the pool panel — a title ("Mixer"/"Super Mixer" + JP subtitle) and an `X / 3` filled-count badge, in both modes, plus Level/Sort filters right-aligned in edit mode only. Removed entirely, not relocated, as a deliberate simplification — one less row of chrome, not a smaller version of it. Level/Sort went through a few more homes after that (MixerBar's own resize-handle row, briefly `TagPoolBody`'s tabs row) before landing in their current one, a Filter popover on the Recommendations header, entirely outside this bar — see "Level/Sort's third home" below for the current state and that full history.

**Pool panel above the slots, not below them — one insertion point, not two.** An earlier version placed the pool panel *below* the slots (between slots and controls) — opening/closing edit mode then had to insert/remove that block in the middle of the bar, which additionally pushed the slots' own downstream neighbor (the controls row) to shift its position relative to the slots twice-removed. With the pool panel at the top instead, opening/closing edit mode only ever inserts/removes one block in one place; the slots row's own position relative to the bar's top never changes in either mode, and only the controls row shifts, exactly as far as the pool panel is currently tall — one visible change instead of a layout that could read as two independent, differently-timed shifts.

Tapping an *empty* slot or the "+ Tag Pool — tap to add tags" affordance (rendered in `KonomiApp`, below `RecommendationsBody`, always visible in browse mode) toggles edit mode — opens it if closed, *closes* it if already open (added after the fact: an empty slot used to only ever open, so tapping one again while already in edit mode was a silent no-op; it now mirrors Done ✓/click-outside exactly, giving a second, already-visible way to close, reachable without moving to the header). A *filled* slot still clears, unchanged either way. This decision is made inside `MixerBar` itself, not `TagSlot` or the reducer — it already receives both `slots` and `editMode`, so its own click-dispatch wraps each `TagSlot`'s handler as `(index) => slots[index] ? onSlotClick(index) : editMode ? onCloseEditMode() : onOpenEditMode()`, rather than threading a new prop through `TagSlot` or teaching the DOM-free `useKonomi` reducer about UI-only edit-mode state. Done ✓ always calls `onCloseEditMode` too, unchanged.

**RECOMMEND only closes edit mode on success — an error leaves the user in edit mode, not the collapsed bar.** This reverses an earlier, explicitly-flagged assumption (closing unconditionally, matching the old `activeTab` behavior) — on reflection, an error like "impossible-min" is something the user needs to *fix*, and fixing it means staying exactly where the tags and section tab already are, not getting bounced back to browse mode and having to reopen the sheet to try again. The mechanism: `useKonomi`'s exposed `recommend()` action now returns the resulting `MixerError` (or `null` on success) synchronously, rather than nothing — `KonomiApp.handleRecommend` only calls `setEditMode(false)` when that return value is falsy. A plain `dispatch` can't answer "did this just error" on its own, since the reducer's state update isn't visible until the next render; `recommend()` (the pure function inside `useKonomi.ts` computing the new state) is called directly against a `stateRef` mirror (kept current via an effect, for the same `react-hooks/refs` reason `MixerBar`'s own ref-mirrors are — see that comment) purely to extract `.error` synchronously, while the actual state update still goes through the normal `dispatch({ type: "RECOMMEND" })` path unchanged. This computes the pure `recommend` function twice per click (once for the synchronous answer, once inside the reducer) — a deliberately cheap, accepted duplication rather than restructuring how the reducer applies its own state, since this scores against a hobby-scale catalog, not a hot loop.

**This is only correct because `recommend` is pure — same `state` in, same output (and same `.error`) out, every time.** The two calls agree by construction *because* neither can see anything except its own `state` argument. If `recommend` ever gained a side effect or any non-determinism (a random tiebreak among equally-scored series, a timestamp, anything read from outside its own argument), the two calls could diverge — the error returned to `KonomiApp` would stop reliably matching the error actually applied to state, silently reintroducing the exact bug this fix closes (edit mode could close on what was actually an error, or vice versa). Flagged directly in a comment at the call site in `useKonomi.ts` so this dependency isn't invisible the next time someone touches `recommend`: keep it pure, or replace this shortcut with something that only computes the result once (e.g. dispatching a precomputed next-state rather than a bare action type).

**Where each retired `PanelToggleHeader`/`TagPoolRecommendationsPanel` piece ended up:** the Recommendations/Tag Pool toggle buttons and their `/` divider have no replacement (there's nothing left to toggle). `resultsHint` + Clear ✕ moved to a new `ResultsHeader.tsx`, rendered unconditionally above `RecommendationsBody` — no more tab-gating needed since there's only one surface. `TagIcon`+`LevelSelector` and `SortIcon`+`SortSelect` moved through several more homes after that (see "Level/Sort's third home" below for the full history) before settling in a Filter popover on that same `ResultsHeader`, alongside Clear ✕. The "drag or tap to load a slot" hint has no direct replacement either — the "+ Tag Pool" affordance's own always-visible label supersedes it.

### Level/Sort's third home: out of MixerBar entirely, into a Filter popover on the Recommendations header — and MixerBar's resize grip moved the opposite direction, into `TagPoolBody`'s tab row

**This section previously described an intermediate design (Level/Sort living in `TagPoolBody`'s own combined tabs+filters row, with a `flex-col-reverse` stacking breakpoint, a `ResizeObserver`-measured row height, and a `filtersCompact`/`FILTERS_ICON_ONLY_WIDTH_PX` circular-dependency fix) that no longer matches the code.** By the time of the move described below, Level/Sort had already been relocated again, to their own row directly in `MixerBar` (a plain `@container`-scoped pair of breakpoints, `@min-[480px]` for labels and `@max-[352px]` for the segmented-control/native-select → single-tap-cycle collapse, no `ResizeObserver` involved) — simpler than the mechanism this section used to document, and itself now superseded too. If you're trying to understand *that* now-removed row's own internals, read this section for the current state instead; the flex-col-reverse/ResizeObserver narrative above no longer describes anything live in this codebase.

**Current state: Level/Sort don't live in `MixerBar` at all.** They're in `ResultsFilterButton.tsx`, a small popover opened from a Filter icon inline with `ResultsHeader`'s "RECOMMENDATIONS" title, next to Clear ✕. `useLevel()`/`useSort()`/`setSortPref()` (`tagPoolPrefs.ts`) are read exactly the same way as always — a global, `useSyncExternalStore` + localStorage-backed store, read independently by `ResultsFilterButton`, `TagPoolBody`, and `ResultCard` — so relocating the *control UI* a third time touched none of those other three; only `handleLevelChange` (`KonomiApp.tsx`, the prune-cascade wrapper around `setLevelPref`) needed re-threading, now passed to `ResultsHeader` → `ResultsFilterButton` instead of to `MixerBar`.

**Level and Sort are stacked (two rows), not side-by-side, inside the popover** — a deliberate change from every prior home, where they'd always shared one row and negotiated width via a horizontal `ScrollFadeArea` fallback. That fallback existed only to share space with whatever else was in the same row (the old header's title/badge, then MixerBar's resize grip/shortcuts); a standalone popover has nothing else competing for its width, so stacking removes the need for it entirely. Each control keeps its own full-form/collapsed-single-tap-cycle dual render (`LevelSelector`/`SortSelect`'s `collapsed` prop, unchanged), but the breakpoint numbers are new: the popover has its own `@container`, and the old `@min-[480px]`/`@max-[352px]` values (calibrated against MixerBar's much wider card) would either never fire or always fire against this narrower box, not a case of reusing a number that happens to still work. The popover itself is a fixed-formula width (`w-[min(340px,calc(100vw-3rem))]`), not `w-max`/intrinsic sizing — confirmed live as a real bug otherwise: an element with `container-type: inline-size` (what Tailwind's `@container` sets) can't size itself from content whose own rendering depends on querying that same container, and browsers resolve the circularity by collapsing the box near-zero width instead, not by "just working it out." 340px was chosen live, from `Element.scrollWidth` on the Tag Density row (294px unpadded content, the wider of the two rows) plus padding and breathing room — comfortably above both collapse thresholds in ordinary use, so the collapsed form exists as a safety net for extreme cases (a very narrow viewport, large text zoom) rather than something normally exercised.

**`LevelSelector`'s hover/tap hint (renders *below* the control, absolutely positioned) needed explicit clearance reserved against the Sort row now directly beneath it** — `pb-[24px]` on the Tag Density row's own wrapper, the stacked-layout equivalent of the padding-then-cancel trick the old shared row used against `ScrollFadeArea`'s own clipping. Confirmed live: without it, the hint would render on top of the Sort row rather than in dead space, since absolutely-positioned content doesn't push flex-column siblings down on its own.

**MixerBar's resize grip/50%/100%/Reset shortcuts moved the opposite direction — in from MixerBar, down into `TagPoolBody`'s own section-tab row — once Level/Sort leaving left that row with nothing else to anchor its own height.** The tab row (`TagPoolBody`'s `rowRef`-wrapped div) is `SectionTabStrip` on the left (`min-w-0 w-fit`, shrinks/scrolls first, unchanged) and a `trailingControls` prop (shrink-0, supplied by `MixerBar`) on the right — the same "scrollable group first, fixed group second, `justify-between`" layout the old MixerBar row used for filters-vs-cluster, just with tabs standing in for filters. `trailingControls` is built in `MixerBar.tsx` (all the drag/reachability state — `handleResizeStart`, `handleRowPointerDown`, `frozenReachability`, `canShowResizeHandleRowDisplay` — stays owned and unchanged there) and passed down as a plain `React.ReactNode`; `TagPoolBody` doesn't know or care what's inside it, only where to place it. `rowRef`'s pre-existing `ResizeObserver` (feeding `chipAreaHeightPx`) needed zero changes to pick up the new content's height — it already measures whatever the row's real rendered height is.

**A new "X" close button (`onClick={onCloseEditMode}`, the same prop `KonomiApp` already passed to `MixerBar` for Escape/outside-click) sits immediately right of the grip, always rendered** — unlike the grip/50%/100%/Reset cluster, which stays gated on `canShowResizeHandleRowDisplay` exactly as before (dragging/snapping something that can't resize anything shouldn't be reachable), the close action must stay reachable even at the narrowest viewports where resizing itself has stopped being useful.

**Why not just patch the old resize-handle row to survive without filters, instead of removing it?** The old row stayed visually stable across `canShowResizeHandleRowDisplay` toggling only because filters — which were *not* gated on it — acted as a height floor even when the grip/shortcuts cluster hid. Once filters left, if only the row's shell had stayed (holding just that cluster), hiding the cluster would newly shrink the row's own measured height, which feeds back into `poolPanelMaxHeight` → `canShowResizeHandleRowDisplay` → the cluster's own visibility — the same "value derived from an element's presence gates that element's presence" oscillation this file has already fought once for the cluster itself (see the seventh-trap section below). Moving the cluster into `TagPoolBody`'s already-self-measuring tab row sidesteps the whole class of bug rather than patching around it: `MixerBar`'s `growableRef` now wraps `dialogRef` alone (no separate sibling row at all), simplifying `growableFixedOverheadPx` down to just `dialogRef`'s own small margin.

**The shortcut cluster itself later shrank from three buttons to one: a single arrow, its target (50% or 100%) chosen by the panel's *current* position, not two independently-gated buttons plus a separate Reset.** Requested directly. Below 50% of `poolPanelMaxHeight`: only the 50% arrow (`ArrowUpHalfIcon`). At 50% or above but short of 100%: only the 100% arrow (`ArrowUpIcon`). At 100%: no arrow at all — nowhere left to jump to. The old Reset/down-arrow button (`ArrowDownIcon`, `handleResetPoolHeight`, `isAtDefaultHeight`) is deleted outright, not hidden — dragging back down by hand via the grip was always available alongside it and remains the only way down now. `isAtHalfHeight` is gone too, folded into the same position check that now picks which arrow to show (`isBelowHalfHeight`) rather than living as a separate "hide the 50% button once we're exactly at half" condition.

**Confirmed live as a real bug in the first pass: a bare `effectiveHeightPx < poolPanelMaxHeight * 0.5` comparison, no tolerance, left the 50% arrow stuck showing forever after the very first tap.** Clicking the 50% snap button sets `effectiveHeightPx` to exactly `poolPanelMaxHeight * 0.5` at that instant — but `poolPanelMaxHeight` itself is a live `ResizeObserver`-driven value, and it can drift by a sub-pixel amount on the very next measurement for reasons that have nothing to do with the user's own action (the same kind of noise `isAtHalfHeight`/`isAtFullHeight`/`isAtDefaultHeight` already carried a 1px tolerance for, elsewhere in this same file, before this pass deleted two of the three). A zero-tolerance strict-less-than flipped back to "still below half" from that drift alone, even though the panel had genuinely already snapped there — reading as the button silently refusing to update rather than anything visibly broken. Fixed the same way the file's other height comparisons already do: `isBelowHalfHeight = effectiveHeightPx < poolPanelMaxHeight * 0.5 - 1`, a 1px dead-band biased toward treating the boundary neighborhood as "at or above half" rather than "still below" — reusing an existing, proven pattern rather than inventing a new one for this one comparison.

**The custom direction-aware wheel/touch exemption for the old filters' own scroll area was already dead code by this point** (see the superseded narrative above for when it was removed) — nothing further changed here with this move, since `TagPoolBody`'s tab row was already inside `dialogRef`'s subtree, covered by `blockScrollThrough`'s existing blanket exemption, both before and after the grip/shortcuts joined it.

**The drag grip itself was a plain `<div role="separator">` (not a `<button>`) from the very start of this bar, well before any of the moves above — changed to `IconButton` in this same pass, requested directly for consistency with the arrow and Close buttons beside it.** A real, reported issue prompted it: dragging the grip immediately after tapping a resize arrow sometimes didn't respond. This was **not reproduced** in this project's own headless-browser testing — a wide matrix of orderings (arrow→drag, drag→arrow, arrow→close→reopen→drag, rapid double-clicks, zero-delay succession) all transitioned correctly, every time, across many repeated runs — consistent with this being a real-device-only input quirk (the same class of thing this file has already hit before: the compositor-desync/WebKit bugs elsewhere in this doc), not a logic bug reachable from synthetic mouse events. Converting the grip to `IconButton size="sm" border="none" backgroundColor="transparent"` (identical props to the arrow/Close buttons) was applied as the best available fix regardless: it gives the grip the same defensive `pointer-events-auto` override `IconButton`'s own base classes already apply to every other icon control on this bar (see that component's comment on why — a button inside a `pointer-events-none` ancestor would otherwise silently stop receiving clicks), which a plain `div` never had. **If a real-device drag failure ever reproduces again after this, the `pointer-events-auto` theory above was not the actual root cause and this needs a fresh look** — `onPointerDown={handleResizeStart}` itself, and everything downstream of it (`isDraggingRef`, the `window`-level move/end listeners, `setPointerCapture`), carried over completely unchanged; only the element it's attached to changed, from a `div` to a `button`. `role="separator"`/`aria-orientation="horizontal"` are preserved via `IconButton`'s own prop passthrough (`ButtonHTMLAttributes` already includes both) — deliberately kept even though the underlying element is now a real `<button>`, since this control still isn't "activatable" the way Close is, only draggable, and the original semantic intent (a resize separator, not an action button) is still accurate. `handleResizeStart`'s own parameter type changed from `React.PointerEvent<HTMLDivElement>` to `React.PointerEvent<HTMLButtonElement>` to match.

**All three trailing controls (the single resize arrow, the grip, and Close) now share one standardized 30×30 hit-box, confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect`** — same width/height, zero gap between adjacent boxes, same y-position, matching Filter/Clear's own 30×30 in `ResultsHeader` too. Requested directly, framed as "wherever you tap inside the box, you'll hit it" — a single consistent affordance size across every icon-sized control this app now has, not just a coincidence of shared props. No padding/gap classes on the row itself changed to get here; only the grip's own element type did.

**The grip's own glyph changed too, in a follow-up: `GripHorizontalIcon` (2 rows × 3 dots, wider than tall, 16×8) was replaced with `GripIcon` (3×3, 9 dots, square, 16×16), requested directly.** `GripHorizontalIcon` is deleted, not kept alongside — confirmed via grep it had exactly one caller (this same grip), so there was nothing else to preserve it for. The old name/shape made sense when this glyph sat alone in a wide, non-square row of its own; now that the grip lives in its own square 30×30 `IconButton` box beside the resize arrow and Close (same box every other icon in that row uses), a square 3×3 grid reads as a more standard "grip" glyph for that context (c.f. Lucide's `grip`/`grip-vertical`) than the flatter 2-row version did. If a future pass ever needs the old flatter 6-dot glyph back (e.g. for a differently-shaped, non-square affordance elsewhere), it's preserved verbatim in git history at the commit that made this change — not worth keeping as dead code in `icons.tsx` in the meantime.

**Clear ✕ (`ResultsHeader.tsx`) got an icon (`CloseIcon`, `icons.tsx`, shared with the new close button above) and the same text+icon dual-render collapse this app already uses elsewhere** (`RecommendButton`'s `RECOMMEND ▸`/arrow swap is the template) — full "Clear ✕" text down to 480px viewport width, icon-only below it. Live-bisected against the header row itself, not assumed: unlike the old MixerBar resize row (which had a real, measurable dead zone if a rung landed wrong), this row's title already has its own independent shrink point (the JP subtitle drop at 417px) and never literally overflows regardless of where Clear's own breakpoint lands — 480px was chosen for a comfortable margin before the row starts to feel tight, confirmed by screenshotting the row across a range down to 350px, not because a narrower number would have broken anything measurably.

**The new Filter button (`ResultsFilterButton.tsx`) needed the same outside-click allowlist entry Clear ✕ already had** (`data-results-filter`, mirroring `data-clear-results`) on `MixerBar`'s own click-outside-closes-edit-mode listener — without it, opening or using the Filter popover while Tag Pool's edit mode happened to already be open would close edit mode as an unrelated side effect, since the popover lives in `ResultsHeader`, outside `MixerBar`'s own `cardRef` subtree that the listener otherwise exempts wholesale.

**Clear's collapsed (icon-only) form is a second, full sibling button, not the same `ActionButton` swapping its own content — requested directly, so it can match the Filter button's exact size.** `ActionButton`'s sizing is padding-based (`px`/`py` around whatever children it's given), with no fixed box independent of content — swapping its children for just `CloseIcon` narrows the button but doesn't change its height, so it never actually matched `IconButton`'s fixed 30×30 `size="sm"` box the Filter button uses. Fixed the same way `LevelSelector`/`SortSelect`'s own collapsed forms already are: two full sibling elements, `hidden`/shown via the same `min-[480px]` breakpoint the old single-button version used internally, rather than one element with conditional content. Clear's collapsed form is an actual `IconButton size="sm"` now (`border="base"`, no shadow, `backgroundColor="var(--ink)"`/`textColor="var(--bg)"` to keep its own established inverted look) — same box, same border treatment as Filter, just its own colors preserved. `CloseIcon` itself was bumped from 15×15 to 16×16 to match `FilterIcon` exactly, since the two now sit directly beside each other.

**Follow-up, same session: Clear's *full-text* form was asked to match too, not just its collapsed icon form — both of Clear's two states are a flat 30px tall now, matching Filter exactly at every width.** This meant changing `ActionButton`'s own `size="sm"` (`SIZE_CLASSES.sm`, `ActionButton.tsx`) from padding-based (`px-space-lg py-space-md`, rendering 36.5px tall) to a fixed `h-[30px]` with flex centering — a plain padding value can't hit an exact px target the way a fixed height can. Safe to change unconditionally rather than adding a variant: `size="sm"` on `ActionButton` has exactly one caller, this same Clear ✕ button (confirmed by grep, documented in that file's own comment), so there was no second caller's sizing to preserve.

**Removed in that same change: `size="sm"`'s own unconditional `inline-flex` was deliberately left out, not an oversight.** A first version added `inline-flex` directly into `SIZE_CLASSES.sm` alongside `items-center`/`justify-center` — confirmed live as a real bug immediately: the caller (`ResultsHeader.tsx`) also needs to toggle *visibility* responsively (`hidden` below 480px, shown above it), and combining an unconditional `inline-flex` from `SIZE_CLASSES` with a `hidden min-[480px]:inline-block` override from the caller's own `className` puts two classes in the same joined string that both declare `display`, with no guaranteed winner — the exact undefined-cascade-order problem `IconButton`'s own `display` prop (see its own comment) exists specifically to avoid. Fixed by removing `display` from `SIZE_CLASSES.sm` entirely, leaving only the alignment utilities (`items-center`/`justify-center`, inert without a flex/inline-flex ancestor but harmless to leave present) — the caller is now the *only* source of `display` for this size, matching `hidden min-[480px]:inline-flex` (not `inline-block`) so the value it switches to is the same one the alignment utilities need. **If `size="sm"` ever gets a second caller that always shows (no responsive toggle), that caller must supply its own unconditional `inline-flex` — this isn't baked in anymore.**

**`--header-row-height` (`globals.css`) and `ResultsHeader.tsx`'s own `min-h`/`mb-[calc(...)]` pair were retuned together, not independently** — both were calibrated against Clear's old 36.5px, and the file's own comment already flagged "these must never both change without checking the other." Re-measured live rather than recomputed by hand: title line-height is still 22.359375px (unchanged), Clear/Filter's shared height is now 30px, so the centering slack is `(30 − 22.359375) / 2 ≈ 3.82px` (was 7.08px against the old 36.5px). `min-h-[36.5px]` → `min-h-[30px]`, `mb-[calc(var(--space-2xl)_-_7.08px)]` → `mb-[calc(var(--space-2xl)_-_3.82px)]`, `--header-row-height: 36.5px` → `30px`. Confirmed live afterward: the measured gap from the title's own bottom edge to the results card's top edge is exactly 24px, matching `--space-2xl` and the mixer's own card→mixer gap, the same invariant this margin existed to preserve in the first place.

**The two rows inside the Filter popover are right-aligned (`justify-end`), not left** — requested directly, no functional reason beyond visual preference; each row's label/icon/control group now hugs the popover's own right border instead of its left.

**Follow-up round: the Filter popover needed to outrank MixerBar's own edit sheet when both are open and visually overlapping — a new `--z-popover` token (`globals.css`, value 20) was added for it, one tier above `--z-overlay` (10).** MixerBar's `cardRef` can grow tall enough (dragged/snapped toward 100%, especially on a short viewport) to visually reach up into the results header where the Filter button and its popover live — already an accepted, intentional consequence of deliberate resizing elsewhere in this doc (see "Pool panel height" below, the masthead-overlap case). Before this token existed, both `ResultsFilterButton`'s own wrapper and `cardRef` were siblings competing at effectively the same z-index tier (the wrapper was `z-raised`, `cardRef` is `z-overlay`, `z-overlay` already wins) — meaning `cardRef`, later in the DOM, would silently paint over the Filter popover and swallow its own clicks whenever they overlapped, confirmed live via `elementFromPoint` before this fix landed.

**Only the *open* popover needs to win, not the closed trigger button — requested directly and separated out from the fix above, not folded into one unconditional z-index.** `ResultsFilterButton`'s wrapper now switches between `z-raised` (closed) and `z-popover` (open) based on its own `open` state, rather than sitting at one fixed value. A nested z-index doesn't escape its own ancestor's stacking context, so this had to be the *wrapper's* own z-index that changes, not just the inner popover panel's — the panel itself no longer needs any z-index of its own at all (removed): it renders only while `open` (so it's always inside the now-elevated wrapper already), and `position: absolute` alone already paints it above its own static-positioned trigger-button sibling within that same local context, with no z-index required for that narrower, purely-local ordering. Confirmed live both ways: with the popover closed, MixerBar's expanded panel fully covers the Filter (and Clear, and the title) row, exactly like any other content in its path; with the popover open first and MixerBar's panel then resized to grow over it, the popover — and a control inside it — stayed visible and genuinely clickable (`elementFromPoint` inside the overlap resolved to the popover, not `cardRef`; clicking a control inside it still worked).

**The Filter icon's own shadow was removed in the same round** (`shadow="elev-2"` dropped from its `IconButton` call) — visual-only, requested directly, no functional change.

**Follow-up: the Filter icon's own colors went through two corrections before landing on the current pairing.** First pass used `--ink`/`--bg` for the active (open) state, matching Clear ✕'s own inverted look — rejected as looking identical to Clear's own color and not theme-adaptive (Konomi/Tide/Newsprint each define their own `--ink`, but it reads as the same dark neutral in every theme, unlike an actual accent token). Second pass moved to `--primary` for the active fill, but the *default* (closed) state still used `--ink` for both icon and border — also corrected, since it's the same "not distinct, not theme-adaptive" problem just for the resting state. **Final: `--primary` throughout, both states** — icon and border are `--primary` by default (outlined, transparent fill), and both invert to `--surface`-on-`--primary` while `open`, the same pairing `LevelSelector`'s own active segment already uses for an "on" state. Deliberately not `--secondary` either, since that's `RecommendButton`'s own accent color (`DEFAULT_ACCENT`, `config.ts`) — reusing it here would make this button read as sharing Recommend's own "primary action" weight instead of its own, smaller one.

**`IconButton.tsx` gained a `borderColor` prop to make the border half of that fix possible** (defaults to `var(--ink)`, preserving every existing caller's look exactly — this component's border color used to be a hardcoded literal class, not a prop at all, the same way `backgroundColor`/`textColor` already were props but border color wasn't). Added as an inline style, not a `className` override, for the identical reason `backgroundColor`/`textColor` already are: an appended `className` with its own `border-color` utility would sit alongside this component's own previously-hardcoded one in the same joined string, and Tailwind doesn't order generated classes by source position (see this file's own `display` prop constraint, documented elsewhere in `IconButton.tsx`) — there'd be no guaranteed winner.

**The "Sort" label inside the popover became "Tag Sort"** (`ResultsFilterButton.tsx`, renders uppercase via the existing `uppercase` class either way) — requested directly, clarifies which sort this is now that it's no longer sitting directly beside a `SortIcon` inside MixerBar's own controls, where the context was more obvious.

### The single box that grows: how edit mode actually expands

**Three architectures, in order, each rejected for a concrete reason found building the next:**

1. **`position: fixed`** (Step 1) — the bar pinned to the viewport bottom, browse-mode content given an explicit spacer (`MIXER_BAR_SPACER`, `pb-[290px]`, sized to the bar's own measured height) so nothing scrolled underneath it. Worked, but made "fit header+cards+mixer in one viewport" (the later `--result-card-height` clamp work, see "Recommendations layout" below) fight against a fixed element that never took part in normal document flow — the whole point of that feature is the page's own scroll behavior, which a fixed bar sits outside of by definition. Un-pinned back to normal flow once that requirement existed.
2. **Two separate boxes** (an overlay for the edit-mode content, sitting above the always-visible mixer card) — tried once the bar was back in normal flow and needed to visually "grow upward" without reflowing the page underneath it. Repeatedly produced a seam/notch/border-mismatch at the shared edge between the two boxes, through several different attempted patches (border-color matching, shadow clip-paths, negative-margin overlaps) — every fix treated the symptom (two independently-decorated boxes meeting) rather than the cause (there were two boxes at all).
3. **One box, `flex-col-reverse` + fixed height + `overflow: visible`** — an intermediate attempt at consolidating to a single element. Abandoned once it turned out to still leak the page's own `--bg` through a `clip-path` used for shadow trimming (`clip-path` clips backgrounds and content too, not just the shadow it was aimed at) — a real bug, not a dead end in the *structural* idea, but enough friction to reach for the simpler mechanism below instead.

**What actually shipped: one box (`cardRef`), a `marginTop` that goes negative in edit mode.** `cardRef` holds everything — the resize-handle row, the tag-pool dialog, the slots row, the controls row — as normal document-flow children, with exactly one border and one shadow drawn once around the whole thing (no shared edge to seam, because there's no second box). Growth happens via:

```
style={{ marginTop: editMode ? -(growableFixedOverheadPx + effectiveHeightPx) : 0 }}
```

`growableRef` wraps just the resize row + tag-pool dialog (the two rows that only exist/have height in edit mode) — nothing else. Algebraically, `cardRef`'s own rendered bottom edge is `naturalTop + marginTop + ownHeight`; since `ownHeight` grows by exactly `growableRef`'s own height when edit mode adds that content, the negative margin exactly cancels it out — the bottom edge (and everything below it: slots, controls) lands at the same page position whether edit mode is open or closed. This is the CSS-flow equivalent of what `position: fixed` gave for free, minus the reflow/viewport-fit conflict Step 1 hit.

**`growableRef` needs `flow-root`, not a plain `<div>`.** A plain wrapper lets its children's own top/bottom margins collapse *through* its boundary, under-measuring the true space they consume by exactly those margins — confirmed live as a real ~46px position-shift bug on opening edit mode (part of it; see the focus/scroll bug below for the rest). `flow-root` establishes a new block formatting context, containing the margins properly.

**Two distinct bugs, not one, produced that 46px shift; both had to be found and fixed.** After the margin-collapse fix above closed part of the gap, a real remainder stayed — traced to the browser's own default focus-scroll behavior: `dialogRef.current?.focus()` (see "Close behaviors" below) briefly renders the dialog partially above the viewport's top edge the instant it becomes visible, before the negative margin's paint has "caught up" — the browser doesn't know this is intentional, so it auto-scrolls the page to bring the newly-focused element into view, which reads as this whole bar visibly jumping on open. Fixed with `preventScroll: true` on both `.focus()` calls (open and close). Confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect` diffed against the page (not the viewport) and `window.scrollY` before/after: slots/controls' own page position never actually changed — this was purely the browser's own focus-scroll, layered on top of the margin-collapse bug, not a second layout bug.

**`relative z-[var(--z-overlay)]` on `cardRef`.** Without an explicit `position` + `z-index`, this box is a plain static element — and a plain static element always paints *behind* any sibling with its own explicit stacking, regardless of DOM order (see "Z-index scale" above, which now documents this as a general rule). Confirmed live as a real bug once edit mode's growth visually overlapped the results grid above it: `ResultCard`'s front face (`position: absolute` + `z-raised`, for its own local flip-face stacking) painted *on top of* the expanded mixer. `z-overlay` is the same token the old `position: fixed` bar already used for its own page-level stacking — reused here, not a new one.

**The resize-drag "glitching," and the fix that generalizes:** see "This is the third time this bar has hit a variant of..." further below — `growableFixedOverheadPx`/`effectiveHeightPx`'s own split exists specifically so a `ResizeObserver`-measured value never gets combined with a synchronously-dragging one in the same commit.

**A related, separately-discovered bug in the same investigation: `SectionTabStrip`'s mount-time `scrollIntoView` scrolled the whole *page*, not just its own tab strip.** `TagPoolBody` (and the `SectionTabStrip` inside it) stays mounted at all times — see "State preservation" below — clipped to zero height via `max-h-0` when edit mode is closed, not unmounted. `SectionTabStrip`'s own effect (`tabRefs.current.get(activeKey)?.scrollIntoView({ ..., block: "nearest" })`) fires on every mount regardless, on the assumption that `block: "nearest"` can never affect the page's own scroll — true only while the strip is actually visible. Confirmed live as a real bug: with the strip clipped to zero height, there's no meaningfully scrollable block-axis ancestor nearby for `"nearest"` to satisfy, so the browser walks up to the *document itself* and scrolls the whole page to bring the (invisible, clipped) button into view — reproduced on every fresh page load. Fixed with a new `visible?: boolean` prop (default `true`, so no other caller needs to change) that gates the effect; `TagPoolBody` threads its own `editMode` prop through as `SectionTabStrip`'s `visible`. Confirmed live via a `scrollIntoView` interceptor with a full stack trace (not assumed from the symptom alone), and confirmed the intended in-panel behavior (bringing an off-screen tab into view via arrow-key nav) still works once the strip is actually visible.

### `position: sticky; bottom-[space-2xl]` — floats when the page scrolls, rests in flow otherwise

Requested explicitly as a replacement for plain normal flow: native browser behavior — pin to the viewport bottom once the page is tall enough to scroll, settle back into ordinary in-flow position (respecting `KonomiApp`'s own trailing `pb-[space-2xl]`) once everything already fits — rather than anything measured/computed in this file. `bottom-[var(--space-2xl)]`, not `bottom-0`: keeps the same minimum gap from the viewport edge the page already guarantees on the left/right (`KonomiApp`'s own `px-[space-2xl]`), so the frame reads as one consistent gutter on every side regardless of which mode the bar is in.

**Investigated live before adopting, three specific risks, all confirmed clear:**

1. **The static-vs-positioned trap** (see `z-[var(--z-overlay)]`'s own comment, above) doesn't reopen — `sticky` is a *positioned* value exactly like `relative` was, so it satisfies the same "must be positioned for an explicit z-index to out-rank a sibling's" requirement that fix already established. Confirmed live: a hit-test at the exact point the grown panel visually overlaps content above it still resolved to this bar's own element, not whatever's behind it.
2. **`KonomiApp`'s own `overflow-x-clip` does *not* hijack the sticky containing block.** Any non-`visible` overflow value technically makes a box a "scroll container" per spec, which raised a real question: does `position: sticky` here resolve against the true viewport, or against that div's own box? Tested with a genuinely tall page (a 2000px spacer): the bar stuck to the real viewport bottom for the entire scroll range and returned to normal flow only once the page ran out — confirmed the true viewport, not the ancestor.
3. **The resize-drag still behaves correctly while stuck.** Dragged the handle up 150px in 30px increments: the bar's bottom edge stayed pinned to the viewport on every single frame, and the panel's height grew in exact 1:1 lockstep with the drag delta, both directions. `handleMove`'s own math (tracking `clientY` deltas in viewport coordinates) is already indifferent to which positioning scheme the box uses.

**`marginTop` (see its own comment, above) stays exactly as it was — computed unconditionally, whether the bar happens to be stuck or not — and that's deliberate, not an oversight.** While genuinely stuck, it's confirmed to be a pure no-op for the bar's *final rendered position*: native `sticky`'s own `bottom` offset determines that regardless of what `marginTop` says (verified live by forcing `marginTop: 0` while stuck and diffing the result — byte-identical to the unforced version). It's tempting to read that as "so skip computing it while stuck" — **this was tried and reverted; see the seventh trap instance below for exactly why.**

### A seventh trap instance, same mount-gating/circular-dependency family as the six above — this one doesn't oscillate or get stuck open, it feeds back into the very thing it measures

`marginTop` has a second job beyond repositioning the box, one easy to miss because it's invisible while stuck: it also cancels the growable content's contribution to the *page's own total layout height* (`position: sticky` does not remove a box from normal flow — only *where it paints* changes, not how much vertical space it's allocated for layout/`scrollHeight` purposes). An attempt to make the "no-op while genuinely stuck" explicit — an `isStuck` state, computed from remaining scroll distance (`document.documentElement.scrollHeight - scrollY - innerHeight`), gating `marginTop` to `0` whenever true — broke this second job: with `marginTop` zeroed, the growable content's real height leaked straight into the page's own `scrollHeight`, which is the exact input `isStuck`'s own formula reads. Opening edit mode could push a bar that was *not* stuck a moment ago over the sticky threshold, purely as a side effect of the un-compensated growth — flipping `isStuck` true (or leaving a stale value) as a consequence of the very change its own value was supposed to gate.

Confirmed live as a real, reproducible, *worsening* regression, not just a theoretical loop: the bar's bottom edge visibly pushed *down* on open instead of the box growing purely upward, and repeated open/close cycles while stuck compounded the drift further each time rather than settling — matching a report of "works fine right after a hard refresh, breaks after cycling through sticky-mode edits a few times." Reverted in favor of leaving `marginTop` unconditional, exactly as it was before sticky existed — a confirmed no-op purely visually whenever the bar happens to be stuck, at the cost of one redundant (harmless) style computation in that case. **A much better trade than a value that can feed back into its own input** — the generalized tell, extending the family above: before gating any value on "is X currently true," check not just whether X's own measurement could later be fed by something that value goes on to affect (the sixth instance's flavor), but whether the thing you're about to stop computing has a *second*, less obvious job the first job's absence would quietly break.

**A related debugging note, worth recording so it isn't rediscovered the hard way: a since-resolved stutter reported during this same investigation traced to stale browser/GPU-process state, not a code bug.** Reported as visible only in sticky mode, reproducible on the reporter's own machine but not via any fresh-process Playwright test (position/frame-geometry sampling, `Performance.getMetrics` layout/style-recalc counts, `sharp`-based screenshot pixel-diffing, and `LayerTree` compositing events were all tried, and all came back clean or identical between sticky and non-sticky). A tab hard-refresh didn't fix it; a full browser **process** restart did. That combination — not reproducible from a fresh process, survives a page reload, cleared by fully restarting the browser — points at accumulated compositor/GPU-process state (plausibly destabilized by this same investigation's own rapid Fast-Refresh churn on a layout-affecting component mid-session) rather than anything in `MixerBar.tsx`/`TagPoolBody.tsx`. Worth checking for this signature specifically before assuming a visual glitch is a code bug: if a fresh browser *process* (not just a fresh tab/reload) makes it disappear, it probably isn't one.

### State preservation: the pool panel is always mounted, never remounted

The pool panel (row 1) is toggled via `max-h-0 overflow-hidden` keyed off `editMode`, not conditional JSX — the exact same trick the retired `TagPoolRecommendationsPanel` used for tab-switching (see "Tag Pool and Recommendations became two tabs" above), generalized from two tab keys to one boolean. This is required, not stylistic: `TagPoolBody`'s own `activeSectionKey` state and every `ScrollFadeArea` scroll position inside it have no persistence outside the live DOM, so unmounting this subtree on every close would reset both, every time. `max-h-0 overflow-hidden` (rather than `hidden`/`display:none`) was the specific choice here: the subtree still lays out normally underneath the clip, so `ScrollFadeArea`'s `scrollHeight`/`clientHeight` measurements stay correct the whole time, with no one-frame `ResizeObserver` catch-up delay on reopen the way a `display:none`→visible transition can have.

### Nothing in this bar has a native scrollbar except the chip area — title, slots, and controls are fixed by construction, not by a scroll fallback

An earlier version gave the bar's own outer container `max-h-[100dvh] overflow-y-auto` in edit mode, letting the whole bar scroll internally on short viewports as a safety net. **This was replaced entirely** — the outer container now carries no scroll/overflow behavior in either mode, and the pool panel wrapper (see below) has no scrollbar of its own either. Two reasons: first, an internal scroll on the bar defaults to `scrollTop: 0`, meaning an overflow would show the *top* (title/slots) and hide the *bottom* (the controls row) until manually scrolled down — backwards from the actual requirement, which is that the controls row must always be reachable, unconditionally. Second, and more fundamentally: title, slots, and controls must **never** scroll, resize, or move, full stop — not "usually don't, thanks to a generous cap," an exact guarantee. That guarantee now comes from clamp math instead of a scroll fallback: the pool panel — the only part of this bar that ever changes size — is bounded so that `(fixed rows) + (pool panel)` can never exceed the viewport in the first place, so there's never a scenario where the outer container would have needed to scroll. See `clampPoolPanelHeight` below.

The only *vertical* scrolling anywhere in this bar is `TagPoolBody`'s own existing chip-area `ScrollFadeArea` (fade gradients, hover arrows, press-and-hold — all unchanged, see "Tag Pool hierarchy" above). Its tab row above the chip area does scroll horizontally too (`SectionTabStrip`'s own `ScrollFadeArea`, unaffected by whatever else has shared this row over time — see "Level/Sort's third home" above for the current occupants), never vertically clipped or resized the way the chip area is. (Level/Sort themselves no longer live in this row at all, and haven't since before the resize grip/shortcuts moved in — see that same section; the paragraph below describing their effect on this row's measured height is historical.)

### Pool panel height: exact, not a cap — 50% of viewport on first open, drag-resizable well past that, persisted

The pool panel does not hug or cap to content — it is **exactly** `effectiveHeightPx` tall, regardless of which section tab is active or how much content that section has. Switching from a long section (e.g. Theme, Otaku level) to a short one (Genres) leaves visible empty space below the shorter section's chips rather than shrinking the panel — confirmed live, panel height measured byte-identical across a tab switch between the two. Only the resize handle (or the very first default) may change this value.

**The resize handle lives at the very top of the edit surface** — not between the slots and the pool panel, where an earlier version placed it. Dragging it only ever visibly resizes the pool panel (title/slots/controls are fixed), but positioning it at the top matches the more natural mental model — "pull this to make the whole panel bigger" — even though mechanically it's still just the one flexible region responding. **Usable at every breakpoint, not desktop-only** — an earlier version hid it below the same 993px tier the controls-row divider uses (mouse/pointer-only, no touch handlers), leaving mobile with no way to resize at all; that gate is gone.

`handleResizeStart` is now Pointer Events (`onPointerDown` + `setPointerCapture`), not the dual mouse/touch listener pairing (`onMouseDown`/`onTouchStart`, then `mousemove`/`touchmove`+`mouseup`/`touchend` on `window`) it used before — that pairing mirrored `ScrollFadeArea`'s own press-and-hold arrows deliberately, to avoid introducing a second interaction pattern for just this one control (that reasoning still holds for `ScrollFadeArea`'s arrows themselves — press-and-hold buttons, not a free vertical drag across the whole viewport, so they don't carry the same risk this control does). Switched here specifically for the capture guarantee: `setPointerCapture` routes every subsequent `pointermove`/`pointerup` for that pointer id straight to the grip, regardless of where the finger/cursor physically travels, and tells the browser this element now owns the gesture — confirmed live (real, trusted mouse-driven events; `hasPointerCapture` reports `true` from the first `pointermove` onward) that capture engages correctly. `touch-action: none` (`touch-none` in the className) and an explicit `preventDefault()` on every `pointermove` are both still kept alongside it, not replaced by it — capture guarantees *where* events go, not that the browser won't independently start its own scroll gesture for a pointer it doesn't yet consider captured, so this is three overlapping defenses for one requirement ("a drag on this handle must never scroll the page behind it"), not three redundant ones. No keyboard equivalent yet — an accepted gap, not solved in this pass, same spirit as the no-focus-trap gap above.

**On first open (no persisted value yet), the default is exactly 50% of the viewport height** — read once via `useDefaultPoolPanelHeight()` (`tagPoolPrefs.ts`), not re-derived on every render. Deliberately *not* live-recomputed on every render: continuously tracking `window.innerHeight` would make the panel silently change size mid-session if the browser window resized before the user ever dragged, contradicting "only the handle changes this." The chosen height persists via the exact same `useSyncExternalStore` + `localStorage` pattern `tagPoolPrefs.ts` already uses for level/sort — `usePoolPanelHeight()`/`setPoolPanelHeightPref()`, no new persistence mechanism. `null` means "never dragged this session, use the 50%-of-viewport default"; once the user drags, a stored pixel value exists and wins from then on.

**Three real bugs surfaced getting this right, in sequence, each masking the next.** First: `clampPoolPanelHeight` (used directly in `MixerBar`'s render body, not only through `usePoolPanelHeight`'s SSR-safe `useSyncExternalStore` path) read `window.innerHeight` completely unguarded — a hard SSR crash (`Error: window is not defined`), not a warning, since `MixerBar` renders server-side too and `window` doesn't exist there. Fixed with a `typeof window === "undefined"` guard, falling back to `Math.max(px, POOL_HEIGHT_MIN)` (no upper clamp) when there's no viewport to clamp against. That fix uncovered the second bug, previously invisible behind the crash: the *default height itself* was a plain `useState(() => window.innerHeight * 0.5)` lazy initializer, guarded the same way — but "guarded against crashing" and "guarded against a hydration mismatch" aren't the same thing. The server has no `window` (falls back to a placeholder), while the client's own *first* render already has a real one and would compute the actual value immediately — two different numbers for the same inline `style={{height}}` attribute (on `TagPoolBody`'s chip-area `ScrollFadeArea`), which React's hydration flags as a mismatch even though that attribute sits inside a visually-collapsed `max-h-0 overflow-hidden` wrapper the whole time — **being invisible doesn't exempt an element's attributes from the server/client comparison**, confirmed live via the exact warning (`style={{height:365}}` vs `style={{height:"60px"}}`) once the crash stopped hiding it.
