Recovering also needed a full dev-server restart, not just fixing the source: Turbopack had already cached the broken generated CSS, so a plain rebuild kept serving the stale error page until the process itself was killed and restarted. Then this exact section of this exact file, written specifically to document that mistake, repeated it: describing the placeholder by spelling out its literal bracketed shape as an example broke the build a second time, since this document is itself project source text the same scanner sweeps. The rule going forward, applied here now by not showing the literal shape at all: never write a real square-bracket-arbitrary-value pattern anywhere in the project as a placeholder or as an example of one, comments and documentation included, describe the shape in plain words instead.

### Row 2 (combined): the slots row and the controls row became one row, requested directly, not a bug fix

`TagSlot` and everything that used to be its own separate controls row now share one row, split into a LEFT group (3 slots, then Tag Pool/Clear) and a RIGHT group (Min Match, Random, a divider, RECOMMEND). This was only possible once `TagSlot` itself dropped its old three responsive height tiers (56px/76px/92px, each with its own stacked caption and index-number content) down to one fixed height, 44.5px, matching `IconButton`/`ActionButton`'s own shared `md` height exactly (see `IconButton.tsx`'s own `SIZE_PX` comment), specifically so it could sit in the same line as those buttons at all, not just visually near them. The content simplified along with the height: a filled slot now shows the tag name as a single truncated line (no more `line-clamp-2` + "TAP TO CLEAR" caption, see the "`line-clamp` clips descenders" section above for where that pattern moved), and an empty slot always shows plain "ADD TAG" text (already the mobile-only fallback before this change, now the only form at every width, the index number is gone for good).

**Combining the rows meant re-bisecting this row's entire breakpoint ladder from scratch, not reusing the old controls row's own numbers.** Three always-visible slots sharing space with everything that used to have a whole row to itself changes the math completely. This first pass (icon tier only, 793/692/445/1070) was itself superseded one round later by a full unified rebuild, see below.

### The controls row is one priority-hide chain, not a set of independently-bisected breakpoints

Requested directly, in a follow-up round: rather than each control picking its own responsive threshold, this row's entire ladder is now ONE ordered sequence, and every rung's own breakpoint is live-bisected to sit exactly where the PREVIOUS rung's own fit stops holding, chained back-to-back, not spaced out by feel. That chaining is the load-bearing part: this round's own first-draft numbers, picked independently per control the way the original icon-tier pass above was, left two real, confirmed dead zones (793-1070px, and later 640-656px) where the row was still wrapping into 2 lines despite something already being hidden, just not enough yet. Re-bisecting each rung against what the PRIOR rung's own content actually measures (not an assumed or reused number) is what closed both gaps and produced a row that's confirmed live to hold single-line continuously from `min-[511px]` up through `min-[1071px]` and beyond, its entire practical range:

- **min-[1071px]: full form.** This row's own pre-existing ceiling (unchanged both times): every control shows its own text label, and the Random/Recommend divider is visible.
- **max-[1070px]: RUNG 1, the divider disappears.** First casualty, before any other control changes, per explicit priority order. Tied directly to the 1071px ceiling, not an arbitrary wide value: confirmed live that hiding the divider alone never actually achieves single-line fit anywhere in the 793-1070px range on its own (the rest of the row is still full-width there), so there's no reason for this rung to start any wider than the one point where the full form itself first stops fitting.
- **(same 1071px threshold): RUNG 2, everything else icon-only, together.** Tag Pool, Clear, Random, and Recommend all drop their text labels at the exact same shared breakpoint the divider disappears at (Recommend's own icon/text swap, previously an independent, unrelated 640px picked in an earlier, different round, now lives inside `RecommendButton.tsx` itself but reads the same 1071px as the rest of this row). Min Match loses only its own "MIN MATCH" text label here, keeping both icons and its full 3-segment control intact, this is what's different from every other control at this rung. The dead zone this round found and closed: leaving these controls at their own old 793px/640px thresholds left the whole 793-1070px range wrapped into 2 lines with only the divider gone, since divider-alone was never enough by itself.
- **max-[750px]: RUNG 3, Min Match itself changes.** The only rung that changes a control instead of just dropping its label: loses the Tag icon (GreaterEqual alone still reads as "match count" beside the segmented/collapsed value next to it) *and* switches from the 3-segment control to the single-tap-cycle collapsed one, together, as one combined step, requested as a single rung, not the two separately-timed steps ("collapse the control" vs. "drop to 1 icon") an earlier round's version used. Placed here specifically because it has to land before Tag Pool starts disappearing (rung 4), per the requested order; the exact width isn't itself forced by an overflow boundary the way the others are (rung 2's own fit already holds comfortably from roughly 657px up to 1070px), so 750px is a clean value inside that already-safe range rather than a bisected edge.
- **max-[657px]: RUNG 4, Tag Pool hidden entirely.** First of the three buttons (Tag Pool, then Clear, then Random) to go. 657px is exactly where rung 3's own fit (Min Match already collapsed, Tag Pool/Clear/Random all still visible) stops holding. This round's own first attempt had this at 640px, copied from an unrelated older number instead of re-bisected against this exact chain, which is what left the 640-656px dead zone.
- **max-[621px]: RUNG 5, Clear hidden entirely.** The width where Tag-Pool-hidden-alone's own fit stops holding.
- **max-[577px]: RUNG 6, Random hidden entirely.** The width where Tag-Pool-and-Clear-hidden's own fit stops holding. Final composition below this point: 3 slots, Min Match (1 icon, collapsed control), Recommend (icon only), nothing left in this chain to hide.
- **RUNG 7 (final): the 3 tag slots scroll horizontally instead of the row wrapping.** Below roughly 490-500px (live-measured, not a bisected breakpoint — see below for why), nothing is left in this chain to hide (Tag Pool/Clear/Random/divider all gone, Min Match and Recommend both at their narrowest forms), so the slots — the last, most expensive thing to give up — start scrolling internally rather than forcing the row to wrap.

**Follow-up round, requested directly: the old `flex-wrap` LEFT/RIGHT 2-line fallback below the final composition is gone, replaced by wrapping the 3 tag slots in a `ScrollFadeArea` (`orientation="horizontal"`, `fitContent`, `restrictTouchAction`), the same treatment `SectionTabStrip` already uses for its own tabs elsewhere in this bar.** The outer row is `flex-nowrap` now, not `flex-wrap` — it genuinely never wraps at any width anymore, confirmed live across a sweep from 1280px down to 375px (this app's own established narrowest-supported width): the row's own `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` stayed equal (no overflow) at every single width tested. `min-w-0` on the slots' own wrapper `div` (not on the `ScrollFadeArea` instance itself, which `fitContent` already sizes to its intrinsic content) is what lets that one flex item shrink below 3-slots-wide once everything else in the row has already claimed its own space — the identical role the old MixerBar filters row's own wrapper played opposite its resize cluster, and `TagPoolBody`'s tab-row wrapper still plays opposite its own trailing controls. Both `Sub-group B` (Tag Pool/Clear) and `RIGHT` (Min Match/Random/divider/Recommend) got an explicit `shrink-0` in the same pass, so the squeeze is guaranteed to land only on the slots, not incidentally on anything else — before this, Sub-group B was usually already empty by the time the slots needed to give way, but that safety was incidental, not explicit.

**Not a bisected breakpoint like the rungs above, because this rung doesn't need one — that's the point of scrolling instead of hiding.** Every rung above exists because something has to be *removed* at an exact width or the row breaks; the slots' own scroll instead absorbs *any* remaining width continuously, so there's no discrete threshold to tune, confirmed by the live sweep finding the transition (roughly 490-500px, where the scroll area's own `clientWidth` first drops below its `scrollWidth`) rather than picking one.

**Adding a new rung to the chain above (rungs 1-6) still means re-bisecting from that point down, not just the one new number**: every threshold above is only correct in reference to which others are already hidden or changed at that exact width, not a value that stands alone. Rung 7 doesn't participate in that re-bisecting at all, by design — it just keeps working, continuously, regardless of what the chain above it looks like.

**A real, doubled gap turned up later between Min Match and Recommend, once Min Match had collapsed and Random had hidden entirely (below rung 6, 577px): reported live as "too much padding."** Root cause: rung 6's own wrapper div nested its `max-[577px]:hidden` hide-entirely condition *inside* a separate outer `min-[1071px]:hidden` div (the icon/text-tier switch), rather than combining both conditions on one div the way Tag Pool/Clear already did. Below 577px, the inner div correctly went `display: none`, but the outer div didn't (its own `min-1071` condition only cares about the 1071px boundary, unrelated to 577px), leaving an empty-but-still-`block` box sitting in the flex row, still collecting its own `gap-lg` on both sides, a full extra 12px of unwanted space (24px total instead of 12px) exactly where Min Match's own collapsed form sits next to Recommend. Tag Pool/Clear never had this problem, since both of their own hide-entirely conditions already live on a single div each, nested only inside the ONE shared icon-tier wrapper they both sit in together, not two levels of nested divs the way Random's used to be. Fixed by merging Random's two conditions onto the same div (`min-[1071px]:hidden max-[577px]:hidden`), confirmed live: the gap measures exactly 12px now, matching every other gap in the row, not 24px.

### `TagSlot` is a fixed 78x44.5px box, always, empty or filled, width no longer flexes with content

`TagSlot` used `flex-1 min-w-[78px]` up through the round above, on the theory that it would grow to fill available space at wide viewports and shrink back down to 78px as things got tight. Confirmed live: it never actually grew past 78px at ANY viewport, even 1400px, flex-1's grow factor had nothing to distribute, because its own parent (the 3-slot wrapper) never received more width from ITS parent than its children's own content needed. That made the whole "min" in `min-w` a coincidence, not a guarantee, and it broke the instant a slot's own content stopped being short, fixed text: once a filled slot could hold a real, wrappable tag name (no longer truncated, see below), that content's own unwrapped, single-line width fed into the FLEX CONTAINER's own intrinsic sizing (a documented flexbox quirk: a `flex-basis: 0%` item still contributes its own max-content size to its container's preferred width, despite basis:0 excluding content from the actual layout math), inflating the 3-slot wrapper itself, and `flex-1` then spread that extra width across all 3 slots, not just the long one. Reported live as a real, visible bug: a slot's own box visibly widening the moment ANY tag was dropped into ANY of the 3, not just its own. Fixed by dropping `flex-1 min-w-[78px]` for a literal `w-[78px]`, sidestepping the whole mechanism: a fixed width can't be influenced by content, full stop, matching what this box already always rendered at in practice, just without the fragile coincidence.

**Height stays fixed too, deliberately, not a min-height that grows for long names.** An earlier version of this same round let a long tag's wrapped text grow the box taller (`min-h-[44.5px]`, up to ~4 lines for the longest real name). Reverted per direct request: an empty and a filled slot being the exact same fixed size means every breakpoint/overflow calculation involving this row only ever has one slot height to reason about, not a second, content-dependent one. The length-tiered `font-size` (`text-sm` down to a new `text-3xs`, 9px) already fits the real catalog's longest tag name ("Cute Girls Doing Cute Things," 28 characters) within the fixed 44.5px box on its own, confirmed live via `scrollHeight` vs. the box's own available content height. `overflow-hidden` is present as a backstop, not the primary mechanism.

**Follow-up, once the 3 slots moved into their own `ScrollFadeArea` (see "The controls row" section above, rung 7): a filled slot's own `shadow-[var(--elev-3-accent)]` (a flat 4px/4px bottom-right offset, no blur) started getting clipped — bottom shadow missing on every filled slot, right shadow additionally missing on whichever slot was currently last/right-most.** Same root cause already documented for the results carousel and `ResultCard` ("`ScrollFadeArea`'s viewport is now unconditionally non-`visible` overflow" below): the scroll viewport's own `overflow-x-auto overflow-y-hidden` clips anything bleeding past its bottom edge unconditionally, and past its right edge once a slot is actually at the true end of the scrollable content. Fixed with the identical pad-then-cancel technique already established there: `pb-[4px] pr-[4px]` (matching `--elev-3-accent`'s own exact offset, not a guess) on the scrollable content inside `ScrollFadeArea`, `-mb-[4px] -mr-[4px]` on the wrapper around it, so the row's own layout footprint (confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect` on the neighboring Tag Pool button, identical position empty or filled) doesn't shift by the padding added to accommodate the shadow.

**A separate, older, already-documented issue — `border-dashed` clipping its own left/right edges on iOS WebKit for the *empty*-slot state — is unrelated to the fix above and wasn't touched.** Raised again this round as "left and right dotted borders don't show on mobile," but this is the same accepted iOS-only rendering quirk this file already flags at `TagSlot.tsx`'s own `border-dashed` line: a WebKit bug in how it paints dashed borders, not an ancestor clipping a bleed the way the shadow issue was. Already confirmed tried and rejected as unfixable via `outline-dashed` (clips the same way) or an SVG-stroke replacement (its own corners slide during a resize) — nothing new here changes that conclusion, since this round's fix only ever addressed `ScrollFadeArea`'s own overflow clipping a *filled*-slot shadow, a different mechanism entirely from a browser's own dashed-border rendering.

**A visual, not layout, gap-matching bug turned up once the divider was compared against RECOMMEND directly: RANDOM's own drop-shadow bleeds into the gap on its side of the divider, RECOMMEND's doesn't.** Reported as "the padding looks uneven between the two sides of the divider." Measured live first, before assuming a code bug: `getBoundingClientRect()` showed the *layout* gap was already exactly equal on both sides (12px, this row's own shared `gap-lg`), so the row's CSS wasn't actually wrong. The *visual* gap still read smaller on RANDOM's side because its own `shadow-[var(--elev-2)]` (`3px 3px 0 var(--ink)`, a flat, blur-free offset copy, used by both the icon and labeled forms) extends 3px to the right, directly into that gap, while RECOMMEND's own shadow bleeds down-right, away from its side of the same gap. This is the same "a visual gap has to match the other gap's *rendered* distance, not just reuse its token" principle documented earlier in this file for two differently-shaped instances (`ResultsHeader`'s title row, `Masthead`'s accent bar), a third shape of the identical underlying lesson: something (there, invisible alignment slack or a decorative element's own size; here, a shadow) occupies real visual space inside a margin that a flat, reused token doesn't know about. Fixed with `ml-[3px]` on the divider itself, giving RANDOM's shadow exactly the room it bleeds into without eating into the clean 12px, confirmed live afterward (`getBoundingClientRect` again): the raw gap is now 15px/12px (left/right), which nets to a matching 12px/12px once RANDOM's own 3px shadow is accounted for.

**`EDIT_MODE_FIXED_ROWS_HEIGHT` (476, `tagPoolPrefs.ts`) was deliberately left unchanged, not re-measured, across either round.** That constant is only ever a one-frame bootstrap fallback before `fixedOverheadPx`'s own live measurement takes over (see that state's own comment), and it's already documented as an intentionally conservative upper bound, not a precise value. Combining two rows into one, and shrinking `TagSlot` from up to 92px down to a fixed 44.5px, can only make the real fixed-rows total *shorter* than before, never taller, so the existing constant remains a safe (if now more conservative than strictly necessary) ceiling, the exact same tradeoff this constant already explicitly accepted elsewhere for unrelated reasons. Confirmed live regardless, not just reasoned through: opening edit mode at 390px width (the same worst-case width this constant was originally derived from) renders correctly on the very first frame, nothing overflows or visibly pops into place.

### `MatchSelector`'s three segments show a hover/focus subtext below the whole control

Same pattern as `ThemeSwitcher`/`LevelSelector`: `"match 1+ tag"` / `"match 2+ tags"` / `"match all 3 tags"` (the last says "all 3," not "3+", with only 3 slots total they're the same requirement, and "all" reads more naturally for the ceiling option).

**Badge background *and* text color reflect fill state**, matching the drop-tag boxes' own empty/filled distinction: `bg-[var(--slot-empty)] text-[var(--disabled-text)]` (matching `RecommendButton`'s own disabled surface/label color) at `0 / 3`, `bg-[var(--fill)] text-[var(--ink)]` (matching `Random ×3`'s own bg/text) from `1 / 3` up.

### The three collapsed single-button controls (`MatchSelector`, `LevelSelector`, `SortSelect`) no longer look like a pressed call-to-action, and now roll like a slot machine on change

**All three collapsed forms switched from `bg-[var(--primary)] text-[var(--surface)]` (the same colors an *active* segment uses in each control's own wide form) to `bg-[var(--surface)] text-[var(--ink)]` (the *inactive* segment's own colors).** Requested directly: a solid, colored single button reads as a pressed/active call-to-action, the same visual language `Random`/`Recommend` use right next to it in the same row, not as a neutral display of "this is the control's current setting," which is what it actually is. Matching the inactive color instead reads as the latter. This removes the only visual feedback a tap used to give (the button's own color no longer changes), which is what motivated the roll animation below.

**`RollingLabel.tsx` is a new, shared component**: a slot-machine-style vertical roll on value change, the old value scrolling up and out while the new one scrolls up from below into the same spot. All three collapsed controls render their own current value through it instead of a plain text node. This went through several rounds of live-confirmed bugs before landing on its current form. Each is worth its own record, since the failure modes were non-obvious and some only reproduced on a real device, not in headless testing:

- **A height mismatch (37px vs. the wide form's own 32.5px) for the exact same font-size/line-height/padding.** Root cause: `RollingLabel`'s own wrapper is `inline-block`, and a `vertical-align: baseline` (the default) `inline-block` reserves extra space below itself in its parent's line box, to leave room for a descender lining up against its own baseline, space this element has no real baseline to justify, since its content is absolutely positioned. Fixed with `align-top` on the wrapper, closing the 4.5px gap entirely.
- **The roll's own trigger mechanism, a CSS `transition` toggled between two classes across a double-`requestAnimationFrame` delay, was confirmed live as genuinely unreliable in two separate ways**: the roll sometimes simply never played at all (landing straight on the destination value, no visible motion), and separately, tapping again before a roll finished could leave it permanently stuck mid-flight (the outgoing value parked at its fully-scrolled-away position forever, `transitionend` never firing, confirmed via direct event-listener instrumentation showing zero `transitionrun`/`transitionstart`/`transitionend` events despite the `translate` value visibly, smoothly interpolating the whole time). A CSS *transition* only animates a property change the browser has already painted a "before" value for, which is why the double-rAF dance existed in the first place (commit, then an actual paint, then only then the "after" state), and evidently that three-step choreography isn't reliably guaranteed across real devices. Replaced with CSS `@keyframes` animations (`roll-out-up`/`roll-in-up`, `globals.css`) instead: a freshly-applied `animation` class has no such requirement, since its own 0% keyframe *is* the before state, baked into the animation itself, simply having the class present the instant a roll starts is enough, regardless of paint/commit ordering. Cleanup (clearing the outgoing value once a roll finishes) moved from `onTransitionEnd`/`onAnimationEnd` to a plain `setTimeout` matching the animation's own declared duration, for the same reason: an event depending on the browser actually dispatching it proved unreliable here, a timer depending only on a duration this component already controls didn't.
- **A brief gray-bar flash was reported specifically on transitions to a *shorter* label** (e.g. Tag Density's "Seasoned" to "Otaku", Sort's "Unpopular" to "A-Z", not others). Root cause: this box was sized from `value` alone (the destination), so rolling to a shorter label shrank the box's own width the instant the roll started, before the outgoing text had scrolled away. The surrounding button/border briefly resized around the new, narrower box mid-animation. First fix: size from whichever of the outgoing/incoming values is longer while mid-roll (valid to compare by plain string length, not a measured pixel width, specifically because this label is `font-mono`/IBM Plex Mono, a true monospace face where every character is the same width).
- **That fix stopped the shrink at the START of the roll, but only moved it to the END**: the box still snapped back down to `value`'s own (possibly narrower) width the instant the roll finished, reported live as looking glitchy and unnatural next to `MatchSelector`'s own collapsed form (always a single digit, so it never had a width to snap in the first place). Animating that snap with a plain CSS `transition: width` doesn't work at all: this box's own width is never an explicit length, only ever browser-computed `auto` (`inline-block`, shrink-to-fit). CSS transitions can only interpolate between two explicit lengths, never to/from `auto`, so the declared transition silently did nothing. The actual fix: stop sizing from the current roll's own two values entirely, and instead size the box permanently from the widest label the control could *ever* show (`sizeToWidestOf`, a new optional prop: `LevelSelector`/`SortSelect` pass their own full label list; `MatchSelector` omits it, since `"1"`/`"2"`/`"3"` are already equal width). The box now never resizes at all, matching `MatchSelector`'s own form instead of trying to smooth over a resize `MatchSelector` never needed to make.
- **A real, reported mobile bug, the same family already diagnosed once in this exact file**: rapidly tapping one of these collapsed controls many times in a row eventually stopped registering taps at all, and later taps landed as if hitting a different element entirely. This is the identical real-device WebKit compositor-desync failure mode documented in "The real mechanism behind the 'mashing an empty slot glitches everything' report" below, whose own root lesson was: don't hand the compositor a sudden, discontinuous change to mishandle. Interrupting one roll mid-flight to immediately start another is exactly that kind of discontinuity. Fixed the same way that earlier bug was: prevent the discontinuity from happening at all, rather than trying to make the animation itself tolerate being interrupted cleanly. Each collapsed control's own `onClick` now gates behind a plain ref (`rollBusyRef`), locked for `ROLL_DURATION_MS` (exported from `RollingLabel.tsx`, so the gate's own duration can't silently drift out of sync with the roll it's protecting) after every accepted tap. A tap that lands while the gate is locked is simply dropped, not queued. Confirmed live via a rapid-click stress test (10 taps at 60ms spacing, well under the 220ms gate): the control settles cleanly every time, landing on exactly the value the accepted subset of taps predicts, never stuck mid-roll.
- **The animating pair (outgoing + incoming spans) is keyed on an incrementing `rollId`, not left keyless**, so React mounts a genuinely fresh element pair for every roll rather than updating an existing one in place. A stable key, reused across two back-to-back rolls, wouldn't reliably restart a CSS animation the browser already considers "the same value" as before; a fresh element always starts its animation from 0%.
- **Idle and mid-roll render as two entirely separate elements, not one span reused across both roles.** An early version reused a single span (showing the resting value at rest, and doubling as the "outgoing" span during a roll), confirmed live as a real, visible bug: the settled span still carried the mid-roll transition, so resetting its own class back to the resting position triggered a second, unwanted animated "bounce back" right after the intended roll had already finished. A plain conditional render (this element unmounts, that one mounts) sidesteps the whole class of bug: a brand-new element has no previous frame for a transition to animate from.

### `RollingLabel` migrated to Motion (motion/react) — step 1 of the planned animation-library migration

Replaced the hand-rolled CSS-keyframe + `setTimeout` state machine above (`outgoing`/`currentRef`/`rollId` state, the `roll-out-up`/`roll-in-up` `@keyframes` in `globals.css`) with `AnimatePresence` + a single `motion.span` keyed on `value` directly, `initial`/`animate`/`exit` set to the equivalent `y: "100%"/"0%"/"-100%"` transform states, `transition={{ duration: 0.22, ease: "easeOut" }}`. `ROLL_DURATION_MS` (220) stays the one source of truth for both the animation's own duration and every caller's `rollBusyRef` timeout, unchanged in value.

**What this removes, and why it's safe to remove, per the same standard as everything else in this file:**

1. **What it did**: `outgoing`/`currentRef`/`rollId` tracked "is a roll in flight, and what's it animating from," so a fresh `key={rollId}` pair could be mounted on every change (a stable key wouldn't reliably restart the browser's own CSS animation for what it considers "the same value" as before); a `setTimeout(ROLL_DURATION_MS)` cleared `outgoing` back to `null` once the roll visually finished.
2. **The bug it prevented, and how to reproduce it**: an earlier, `transition`-based (not keyframe) version of this same roll was confirmed live, twice independently, to sometimes not play at all (landing straight on the destination value, no visible motion) — root cause, a CSS `transition` needs the browser to have already painted a genuine "before" value, which React's own commit timing (approximated via double-`requestAnimationFrame`) couldn't reliably guarantee on a real device.
3. **Why Motion is believed to handle it now**: this isn't a "Motion is smarter about timing" claim — it's that the failure mode structurally can't occur under Motion's model at all. `AnimatePresence` + a keyed `motion.span` always explicitly declares its own `initial` (before) and `animate`/`exit` (after) states as data, not as a browser-painted frame to race against; there's no double-rAF choreography for real-device timing to defeat. Confirmed live (not just reasoned): sampled `getComputedStyle(...).transform` mid-roll and found both the exiting and entering spans genuinely present with distinct, non-identity `translateY` values simultaneously, and exactly one span with `transform: none` once settled — the roll reliably plays and reliably completes.
4. **How to restore the old mechanism if this resurfaces**: the pre-migration implementation (the `outgoing`/`rollId`/`useEffect`-pair state machine, and the `roll-out-up`/`roll-in-up` keyframes) is the version immediately prior to this entry's own commit — `git log`/`git show` on this file and the corresponding `globals.css` keyframes at that commit has the complete working implementation to revert to.

**What was deliberately *not* removed, because it wasn't independently re-confirmed, not because it was assumed unnecessary:**

- **`rollBusyRef`** (in each caller — `MatchSelector.tsx`/`LevelSelector.tsx`/`SortSelect.tsx`) still gates a second `onChange` from firing before `ROLL_DURATION_MS` has elapsed since the last accepted one, guarding against the real-device WebKit compositor-desync bug described just above (rapid taps eventually stop registering, or land on the wrong element). Re-ran that bug's own original repro against the Motion-based roll — a rapid-tap burst at a fixed interval well under 220ms — and confirmed tap-by-tap the gate still accepts/blocks exactly as designed (accept, block, block, accept, ...), with the control fully responsive afterward. Motion's own interruption handling was not assumed sufficient to replace this guard; it simply wasn't tested to failure, so the guard stays.
- **The DOM-swap-causes-spurious-blur risk** (`MatchSelector`/`LevelSelector`'s own `supportsHover` gating on their hint's `onBlur`/`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave`, guarding against `RollingLabel`'s child DOM swap triggering a spurious blur on the ancestor button mid-roll) is **unchanged and still necessary** — checked, not assumed away: `AnimatePresence`'s `key`-based swap is still a genuine mount/unmount of a new DOM node, the same underlying mechanism the original bug depended on, not a persistent node Motion morphs in place. Re-tested the touch-hint interaction directly (tap shows the hint, a second tap mid-roll-territory keeps it visible, an outside tap dismisses it) and it holds, but that's because the `supportsHover` gating is still doing its job, not because the risk disappeared.

### Card enter/exit + stagger migrated to Motion (motion/react) — step 2 of the planned animation-library migration

Replaced the hand-rolled CSS-keyframe + exit-before-unmount machinery in `ResultCard.tsx`/`RecommendationsBody.tsx` with a `motion.div` (the same inner perspective wrapper that already carried `[perspective:1100px]`, never the outer `<article>` — that constraint is unchanged and unrelated to this migration, see "Carousel click-through vs. touch-scroll-chaining" above) plus `AnimatePresence` wrapping `RecommendationsBody`'s card map directly. `CARD_ANIM_DURATION_MS`/`CARD_ANIM_STAGGER_MS`/`CARD_ANIM_DISTANCE_PX` (`cardAnimTuning.ts`) are unchanged in value; `CARD_ANIM_EASE_IN`/`CARD_ANIM_EASE_OUT` changed representation only, from a CSS `cubic-bezier(0,0,.2,1)` string to the equivalent `[0, 0, 0.2, 1]` tuple Motion's `ease` takes directly.

**What this removes, and why it's safe to remove, per the same standard as `RollingLabel`'s own migration above:**

1. **What it did**: `RecommendationsBody`'s own `displayedResults` state lagged one render behind the real `results` array specifically so a Clear ✕ had something to keep rendering (and animating out) after `useKonomi.ts` had already removed those entries from `results` (that reducer is deliberately DOM/animation-free). `exitingTitles` tracked which titles were mid-exit; a `setTimeout` computed from `getAnimatingPaceCount`/`getEffectiveStaggerMs` and the declared duration/stagger finalized the clear (syncing `displayedResults` back to `results`) once the exit "should" have visually finished. `result-card-enter`/`result-card-exit` (`globals.css`) were the actual keyframes, applied via `.animate-card-enter`/`.animate-card-exit`, with duration/delay/easing/distance passed in as inline style per card since Tailwind's scanner can't see a JS-interpolated class.
2. **The bug it prevented, and how to reproduce it**: without some deferral mechanism, a Clear ✕ would instantly unmount every removed card the moment `results` went empty, since a plain `.map()` over `results` has nothing left to render for them at all — there'd be no exit animation to see, just an instant disappearance. Reproduce by comparing against a build with `displayedResults` removed and `results` mapped directly: Clear ✕ makes every card vanish in the same frame, no sink/fade.
3. **Why Motion is believed to handle it now, confirmed not just reasoned**: `AnimatePresence` wrapping `results.map()` directly is built for exactly this — when a keyed child (`series.title`) disappears from the mapped array, `AnimatePresence` holds that child's last-rendered element in place (still occupying its grid slot) and plays its `exit` variant before actually removing it from the DOM, calling `onExitComplete` once every currently-exiting child has finished. That callback is what now finalizes the clear (`isBatchClearActive` reset to `false`), replacing the hand-computed `setTimeout` duration estimate with the animation's own real completion signal — confirmed live via the Playwright suite below: cards remain mounted and queryable immediately after a Clear ✕ click, the empty-state placeholder stays hidden throughout, and both fire/resolve at the expected point after the exit actually finishes, for both a full clear (nothing locked) and a partial one (some cards locked, only the unlocked ones exit).
   - A subtlety specific to this app, confirmed by construction, not just by testing: today, only Clear ✕ has ever exit-animated a departing card — a RECOMMEND or a manual swap replaces a card's title outright with no exit animation for the one it replaces (only the incoming card's own entrance ever played). `AnimatePresence` has no built-in concept of "why" a keyed child disappeared, so preserving that asymmetry required `ResultCard`'s `exit` to be a *dynamic* Motion variant (a function, not a plain object) driven by `custom` on the `AnimatePresence` itself (`isBatchClearActive`, true only for the duration of an actual Clear ✕). Motion re-supplies whatever `custom` value `AnimatePresence` currently holds to an already-exiting child at the moment it evaluates that child's exit variant — this is what lets a single `ResultCard` distinguish "I'm leaving because of a Clear ✕" (real sink+fade-out, using this card's own `exitDelayMs`, frozen from its last real render) from "I'm leaving because RECOMMEND/swap replaced me" (`{ transition: { duration: 0 } }`, an effectively instant removal, matching the existing asymmetry) — despite neither being knowable at the time of that card's own last render, only in AnimatePresence's own subsequent one.
4. **How to restore the old mechanism if this resurfaces**: the pre-migration implementation (`displayedResults`/`exitingTitles`/`lastClearGen`'s dual-purpose version, the `useEffect`+`setTimeout` finalizer, `result-card-enter`/`result-card-exit` and their classes in `globals.css`) is the version immediately prior to this entry's own commit — `git log`/`git show` on `RecommendationsBody.tsx`, `ResultCard.tsx`, and `globals.css` at that commit has the complete working implementation to revert to.

**What was deliberately *not* removed, because it wasn't independently re-confirmed, not because it was assumed unnecessary:**

- **`newTitles`-gated entrance (`useKonomi.ts`/`RecommendationsBody.tsx`/`ResultCard.tsx`'s own `isEntering` prop) stays exactly as before** — a card's rise+fade-in is still gated on `newTitles.includes(title) || isEnteringFromSwap`, not derived from Motion's own key-based mount detection, even though a locked/carried-forward card never remounts (same `key={series.title}` across a RECOMMEND) and so would architecturally never replay an entrance under Motion's `initial`-only-applies-at-genuine-mount-time rule regardless of any flag. That reasoning held up under direct verification — a Playwright repro locking a card, then hitting RECOMMEND again, confirmed the locked card's own `motion.div` DOM node (tagged with a marker attribute before the second RECOMMEND) survives unchanged, at rest, with no remount and no entrance replay — but `isEntering` was left in place anyway, layered on top rather than removed, matching this file's own standing rule for any guard whose removal hasn't been confirmed on a real device yet, not just in headless Playwright. Revisit removing it only after that same repro (and the swap/RECOMMEND paths generally) has been confirmed on-device across at least one full step-3 cycle, per the migration plan's own removal-discipline section.
- **`justSwappedIndex`'s forced `enterDelayMs = 0` for a swap-driven single-card entrance** is unchanged — still needed regardless of the `newTitles` question above, since without it a swap on a card at a nonzero row index would otherwise wait through that many stagger steps for no reason (confirmed via the same Playwright suite: the swapped-in card's opacity is already animating well before a full stagger step at its row position would allow, matching the intended zero-delay behavior).
- **`getVisibleCardTierCount`/`getAnimatingPaceCount`/`getEffectiveStaggerMs` (`cardAnimTuning.ts`)** are untouched — still plain JS math computing the same pace/stagger numbers every render; only *where* their output now feeds changed (a Motion `transition.delay`, computed fresh every render and closed over by each card's own `exit` variant function, rather than a raw CSS custom property read once at the moment `isExiting` flipped true).

**`MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"` (`app/layout.tsx`), wrapping the whole app**, replaces the old per-component `window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` checks (there was one, inside the now-removed `setTimeout`-duration effect) — Motion's own documented split under this mode (transform/layout animations disabled outright, opacity/backgroundColor still animate) is exactly the "instant OR simple fade, no motion" behavior this app's card-animation feature was already scoped to support. Confirmed live, not just via the docs: with `reducedMotion: "reduce"` emulated, a freshly-entering card's `computedStyle.transform` reads `none` (or the identity matrix) throughout, while its opacity still animates up to `1` — no motion, but not an abrupt instant snap either.

**Also re-verified, not assumed, since it's the single most important constraint on this whole file**: the carousel's native horizontal swipe-to-scroll still gesture-chains correctly with `motion.div` as the animated wrapper. Tested via a real CDP touch-event drag (not a synthetic wheel/scroll call) starting over a card's tag area at a carousel-tier viewport width, confirming `scrollLeft` actually advances — the same real-device-confirmed touch path this file has documented extensively elsewhere (see "This alone got desktop... fully native everywhere" in `ResultCard.tsx`'s own comment) still works with Motion driving the wrapper's animation.

### A regression from the above, reported live on both desktop and (per a follow-up correction) some mobile browsers too: swapping a card that isn't in the carousel's first/focused position visibly shifts the whole row's scroll position by about one card-width

Reported directly: at a carousel-tier width narrow enough that not all 5 cards fit in the viewport at once, swapping the *focused* (first-visible) card worked as expected, but swapping an *adjacent* card — still fully visible on screen, just not the anchor/focused one — swapped its content correctly and then also shifted the carousel's scroll position over by roughly one card, as if focus had moved to the next card for no reason.

**Ruled out first, based on a plausible-sounding alternative theory** (that a swap might be incorrectly sharing the RECOMMEND-only "scroll to `firstNewIndex`" reset): checked `useKonomi.ts`'s own `replaceCard` reducer directly — confirmed it does **not** touch `recommendGen`/`firstNewIndex`, and `recommendGen`'s own comment on `KonomiState` already states this is deliberate ("never on a single-card REPLACE_CARD swap"). `ScrollFadeArea.tsx`'s own scroll-reset effect only depends on `scrollResetKey`/`scrollResetIndex` (`recommendGen`/`firstNewIndex`), so it structurally can't be firing on a swap. This wasn't the mechanism, even though it was a reasonable guess from the symptom alone.

**Root cause, confirmed via direct instrumentation, not just reasoned through**: a `MutationObserver` on the row (childList) during a swap showed the row briefly holding **6** `article[data-title]` elements — both the outgoing and incoming card simultaneously — for one tick before settling back to 5. This is `AnimatePresence`'s own exit lifecycle: even a `transition: { duration: 0 }` exit (see the migration entry above — this app's own deliberate "instant" removal for a RECOMMEND/swap replacement, as opposed to Clear ✕'s real sink+fade-out) isn't literally synchronous with the state update that triggered it; Motion still keeps the outgoing card's whole subtree — including its `<article>`, the actual flex/grid-participating box — mounted for at least one more tick before finishing the removal. That extra sibling, genuinely occupying flex/grid space for that one tick, is exactly the kind of transient layout perturbation this app has already hit and fixed once before, for a different scroll container: `globals.css`'s `overflow-anchor: none` on `html`/`body` (see "`overflow-anchor: none` already covers scroll-anchoring, but not this" above) — except that fix only covers the *page's own* vertical scroll; the carousel's own horizontal `scrollRef` (`ScrollFadeArea.tsx`) was never covered, because nothing in it produced a transient extra sibling until this migration.

**Fix**: `ResultCard.tsx` now reads two new pieces of state — Motion's own `useIsPresent()` (true for a normal render, false the instant this card leaves `RecommendationsBody`'s `results` array and enters its AnimatePresence-held lingering phase) and a new `IsBatchClearActiveContext` (mirrors `RecommendationsBody`'s own `isBatchClearActive`, but via React Context rather than a normal prop — see that constant's own comment for why: a card mid-exit is no longer present in `results.map()`'s output, so there's no fresh element for it to receive an updated *prop* from at all, but it's still a genuinely mounted descendant of the Provider, so Context still reaches it). When a card is exiting for any reason *other* than Clear ✕ (`!isPresent && !isBatchClearActive`), a `useLayoutEffect` pops its `<article>` out of document flow entirely — plain inline `position: absolute` at its own last measured `getBoundingClientRect()` (relative to the row, which is now `position: relative` for exactly this reason), `pointer-events: none` — so it stops occupying flex/grid space for whatever's left of its lingering tick, regardless of how long that actually takes. A Clear ✕ exit (`isBatchClearActive` true) is left completely untouched — still `position: static`, still occupying its slot throughout the sink+fade, exactly matching the already-verified partial-clear behavior (locked cards' positions undisturbed while unlocked ones visibly exit in place).

This deliberately does **not** touch the `<article>`'s own transform/compositing behavior — it's a plain CSS `position` change on a plain DOM element, not a Motion prop, and not the same mechanism as the transform-on-the-gesture-participating-box issue documented at length elsewhere in this file (`ResultCard.tsx`'s own comment on why perspective moved off the `<article>`). `position: absolute` alone doesn't establish a stacking context or force compositing the way a `transform`/`will-change` does, so this doesn't reopen that risk.

**Verified directly, not just via reasoning**: re-ran the same `MutationObserver` instrumentation after the fix — the row still briefly holds 6 `article` elements (Motion's lingering-removal tick itself isn't eliminated, and doesn't need to be), but `row.scrollWidth` now stays byte-identical across the whole transition (sampled every `requestAnimationFrame` tick) — the 6th element's presence no longer perturbs the row's layout at all. Separately confirmed via Playwright: a Clear ✕'s cards never read `position: absolute` at any sampled point across a full exit (110 samples across the whole animation), and every card settles back to plain `position: static` with no leftover inline style once a swap's transition finishes. This is a leading-hypothesis fix for a bug that (per its own report) only reproduces on real hardware, not reliably in headless Playwright (an initial headless repro attempt showed no `scrollLeft` drift at all, consistent with this app's established pattern of real-device-only paint/layout-timing bugs) — needs on-device confirmation before this is considered closed.

### Two more bugs surfaced in the round after the above shipped, both since confirmed fixed via real-device stress testing, not just reasoned through

**Tag Density's own collapsed hint didn't close on "click off" the way `MatchSelector`'s identical-looking hint did, despite the two controls' code being byte-for-byte the same pattern (`onFocus`/`onBlur` toggling a `shown` boolean).** The difference was never the code, it was each control's own neighbors. `MatchSelector` sits in the main controls row, surrounded by other `<button>`s (Tag Pool, Clear, Random, Recommend); `LevelSelector`/`SortSelect` sit inside the pool panel's own resize-handle row, surrounded mostly by non-form-control chrome (a plain "GENRES" text heading, empty space around chips). `onBlur` only fires reliably when the *newly* tapped target is itself focusable. Tapping another button reliably transfers focus (and blurs the old one); tapping plain text or empty space doesn't always, a real, known mobile Safari/WebKit inconsistency this app hadn't run into before because nothing else in it relied on blur-to-dismiss next to non-interactive content specifically. Confirmed live (headless) that a real click *does* correctly blur/close the hint when the click lands on another button. The persistent-hint reports only ever involved tapping non-button chrome nearby.

**Fix: a document-level `pointerdown` listener on both `MatchSelector` and `LevelSelector` that closes the hint whenever a pointerdown lands outside the control's own wrapper, layered on top of the existing `onBlur` wiring, not replacing it.** Worth flagging as *not* the cleanest possible pattern: this is a second, redundant mechanism doing overlapping work with `onBlur` (both can independently close the same `shown` state), which is duplication a stricter read of "one clear owner per piece of state" would flag. It was chosen anyway because `onBlur`'s own reliability here depends on *what the user happens to tap next*, a condition this component can't control or predict, while a document-level listener closes correctly regardless of whether the tapped target is itself focusable. Added to `MatchSelector` too, even though it wasn't the one reported broken, specifically so both controls share the identical, fully-robust mechanism rather than one being "usually fine because of what's nearby" and the other having the real fix. Recall this if either control's own surrounding markup ever changes (e.g., `MatchSelector` someday sitting next to non-button content the way `LevelSelector` does): the document-listener is why it would stay correct regardless.

### A third, opposite-direction hint bug: the subtext sometimes doesn't show at all, even though the roll animation plays — `onBlur`/`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` weren't gated to hover-capable devices

Reported live as intermittent and hard to reproduce on demand — worked "almost always," failed occasionally, no exact repro steps other than "a bunch of unrelated interactions elsewhere first" (swap a result card, add tags, RECOMMEND, swap/lock/swap again, then tap MIN MATCH). Neither collapsed control (`MatchSelector`/`LevelSelector`) gated its `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave`/`onBlur` behind `supportsHover` the way `ResultCard`'s lock/swap hints already do — all three fired unconditionally, on touch and hover-capable devices alike.

**Leading hypothesis, matching a mechanism this exact file has already confirmed once, in `ResultCard`'s own lock icon**: `RollingLabel` restructures its own DOM on *every* value change — an idle `<span>` unmounts, a two-span rolling pair mounts in its place, then 220ms later the idle span remounts again — and this restructuring happens directly under wherever the tap/click just landed, since the collapsed button doesn't move. Swapping a child element out from under an active touch point is the exact mechanism already confirmed (via real-device Web Inspector logging) to cause a spurious blur/mouseleave on the *ancestor* button in `ResultCard`'s own history (`LockClosedIcon` ↔ `LockOpenIcon`). If the same thing happens here, that spurious blur fires `setShown(false)` right as (or just after) the roll starts — the roll itself still plays (nothing about it depends on `shown`), but the subtext hides at the same moment, reading exactly as reported: "the animation happens... but the subtext doesn't show." The "hard to reproduce reliably, worked almost always" character fits a real-device paint/compositor-timing race rather than a deterministic logic bug — the same class this file has already hit and confirmed multiple times (the compositor-desync family below, the lock icon's own spontaneous-blur timing) — and per that same history, this class of bug does **not** reproduce in headless/emulated Chromium, only on a real device.

**Fix, applied identically to both controls (requested directly — "anywhere we use the single selector")**: gate `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave`/`onBlur` to `supportsHover` (a hook extracted from `ResultCard.tsx` into its own `useSupportsHover.ts`, previously private to that file, now shared by three callers). `onFocus` stays unconditional — it's still touch's only way to show the hint in the first place. On touch, dismissal was already fully covered by the existing document-level `pointerdown` listener above (device/timing-independent by construction), so removing `onBlur`/`onMouseLeave`'s role there isn't a regression — it's one less mechanism racing to control the same state, not a missing one. `SortSelect`'s collapsed form was left untouched: it has no hover-subtext hint at all (no hint data for sort options), so it was never exposed to this bug.

Confirmed live (headless) that this introduces no regression: hover still shows/hides the hint correctly on a simulated mouse device, and repeated taps on a simulated touch device (each one triggering `RollingLabel`'s own roll) keep the hint visible throughout, dismissing correctly only on an outside tap. The actual reported failure mode — real-device touch/paint timing — can't be verified outside a real device, so this is the leading-hypothesis defensive fix, not a confirmed-reproduced-then-fixed one; worth a on-device retest of the original repro sequence to confirm.

**That fix immediately introduced a real regression on touch: the hint stopped showing at all, on any tap.** Root cause, found by comparing directly against `ResultCard.tsx`'s own lock/swap buttons (the pattern this fix was modeled on): those buttons' `onClick` handlers explicitly call `e.currentTarget.focus()` before doing anything else, because mobile Safari/Chrome don't reliably give a plain, already-mounted `<button>` real focus from a tap the way a mouse click does — a fact this file already documents elsewhere, just not connected to this control before now. `MatchSelector`/`LevelSelector`'s own `handleCollapsedClick` never did this. Before gating `onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave` behind `supportsHover`, touch's own synthesized `mouseenter` event (part of the standard touch-to-mouse compatibility shim) had been the *actual* mechanism making the hint appear on a tap — `onFocus` was never reliably firing on its own, it just happened to not matter, since `onMouseEnter` was quietly doing the job instead. Removing `onMouseEnter` for touch, without also making `onFocus` fire reliably there, left touch with no working trigger left at all. Fixed by adding the same `e.currentTarget.focus()` call `ResultCard.tsx` already uses, in both `handleCollapsedClick` functions, restoring the hint on tap without reintroducing `onMouseEnter`'s own DOM-swap vulnerability. Confirmed live (mobile emulation): tap shows the hint, a second tap (cycling the value again) keeps it visible, and an outside tap still dismisses it correctly, on both `MatchSelector` and `LevelSelector`; confirmed unaffected on desktop (hover, and click-through-multiple-values, both still show the hint at every step).

**A separate, unconfirmed-until-this-round bug: rapid interaction with Tag Density/Sort (opening/closing edit mode, switching pool tabs, tapping the controls) intermittently caused the same real-device WebKit compositor paint/hit-test desync this file already documents once below** ("The real mechanism behind the 'mashing an empty slot glitches everything' report"). Never reproduced in headless/automated testing, matching every other instance of this exact bug family in this app. The fix here was necessarily a best-hypothesis defensive change, not a directly-verified root-cause fix, and is now confirmed live by the user after deliberate stress testing (repeatedly opening/closing edit mode, switching tabs, and hammering the collapsed controls). The leading hypothesis: `LevelSelector`/`SortSelect` live inside the resize-handle row, which fully *unmounts* on every edit-mode close (`{editMode && (...)}` in `MixerBar.tsx`), unlike `MatchSelector`, which never unmounts, sitting in the always-present controls row instead. Closing edit mode while a roll's CSS keyframe animation (or the hint's own opacity transition) is still mid-flight hands the compositor exactly the kind of sudden, discontinuous change ("The real mechanism..." section's own root lesson) that class of bug needs. The actual change made: `pointer-events-none` added to every one of `RollingLabel`'s animated/decorative spans, so nothing about an in-flight (or just-finished, not-yet-unmounted) roll can ever be hit-tested, only the real `<button>` around it can. Flag this one specifically if it ever needs revisiting: it's a plausible, not a proven, fix for a bug this app still can't reproduce on demand. If it recurs, the next thing worth trying is preventing the resize-handle row from unmounting while either control has an active roll or visible hint in flight, rather than adding more defensive CSS to `RollingLabel` itself.

## MixerBar's edit panel now opens/closes with a smooth animation, not an instant snap

Requested directly: opening/closing the edit sheet, and later the resize-arrow shortcuts (50%/100%/reset), all used to snap instantly between states. `cardRef`'s own `marginTop` (the mechanism that lets the whole bar grow upward without moving the slots/controls row beneath it — see "The single box that grows" above) and `dialogRef`'s own height toggle were both plain, untransitioned inline styles/class swaps.

**Why this needed more than just adding a CSS `transition`, confirmed by tracing the surrounding code before touching it:**

1. **A `ResizeObserver` already watches the exact elements being animated.** `fixedOverheadPx`'s own effect (`useLayoutEffect`, keyed on `editMode`) runs a `ResizeObserver` on both `cardRef` and `dialogRef`; `growableFixedOverheadPx`'s own effect does the same on `growableRef` (which wraps `dialogRef` alone now). If `dialogRef`'s real rendered height animates smoothly instead of snapping, both observers fire many times *during* the transition, each computing a wrong intermediate overhead value and feeding it back into `poolPanelMaxHeight` → `effectiveHeightPx` → the very values driving the same transition.
2. **The transition must not fire during an active resize-drag or a snap-click**, both of which also change `marginTop`/height on every tick/click and were already instant and responsive; leaving a transition unconditionally active would smooth/delay those too.
3. **Relying on `transitionend` to know when the animation finishes is an already-known-bad pattern in this app** — see `RollingLabel.tsx`'s own comment: animation-completion *events* depend on the browser actually dispatching them, confirmed unreliable enough on real devices here to avoid; a `setTimeout` tied to the same declared duration doesn't have that dependency.

**The mechanism, all in `MixerBar.tsx`:**

- `EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS` (280) drives every transition's `transitionDuration` via inline style, not a Tailwind `duration-[Npx]` class — this codebase's scanner can't see a template-interpolated class (the same constraint documented elsewhere for `border-[length:var(--border-${border})]`-style attempts), and `RollingLabel.tsx`'s own `ROLL_DURATION_MS` already established the fix: one JS constant referenced everywhere a duration is needed, so a CSS duration and a JS timer can't silently drift apart.
- `dialogRef` animates a real `height` (not `max-height`) toward `effectiveHeightPx` — the exact number this file already trusts as the pool panel's own target, not a fresh guess — with `overflow-hidden` now permanent (open and closed alike). **`max-height` was tried first and confirmed live as a real bug specifically for *shrinking* transitions** (the 100%→default down arrow, or any decreasing resize snap): `TagPoolBody` receives `effectiveHeightPx` directly as its own `poolPanelHeightPx` prop with no transition of its own, so its internal chip-area height snaps to the new, smaller target the instant React commits — before `dialogRef`'s own CSS transition has shrunk at all. With `max-height`, the moment that already-shrunk content becomes smaller than the still-transitioning (still large) ceiling, `dialogRef`'s own rendered size collapses to match content immediately, and the max-height transition becomes irrelevant for the rest of its own declared duration. Growing transitions never hit this (content is always already at its full new size from the first frame, so the ceiling stays the binding constraint throughout), which is why the bug only ever showed up shrinking. A hard `height` has no such escape hatch — the box is always exactly the CSS-interpolated value regardless of what content wants to be — so `growableRef`'s own true rendered height, and therefore `cardRef`'s required `marginTop` compensation, now changes smoothly and predictably in both directions.
- `cardRef`'s `marginTop` gets a matching transition (same duration/easing).
- `dialogRef`'s own `marginBottom` (`var(--space-xl)` while open, `0` while closed) is animated *together with* `height` (`transitionProperty: "height, margin-bottom"`), not toggled instantly — confirmed live as a real, separate bug otherwise: toggling it instantly while height animated smoothly meant `growableRef`'s own true rendered height (height + margin-bottom) took a sudden step on top of the smooth height change, with no matching step in `marginTop`'s own transition, drifting the two out of sync for the transition's duration and reading as the slots/controls row briefly shifting (~4px, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect`) before snapping back at the end.
- **Both transitions are gated on `isEditModeTransitioning || isSnapTransitioning`, not `dragHeightPx === null`.** An earlier version used the latter (reasoning that it was the only other thing changing these values every tick) and missed that `handleSnapPoolHeight` *also* sets `dragHeightPx` to `null` (a discrete snap isn't a drag either) — that version made the resize shortcuts' own click incorrectly pick up the transition too, confirmed live as a reported regression ("resize arrows glitch... continuous instead of instant") before the snap-click animation was itself requested. `isEditModeTransitioning` (state) flips true the instant `editMode` changes; `isSnapTransitioning` (separate state) flips true directly inside `handleSnapPoolHeight`/`handleResetPoolHeight`. Kept as two flags, composed with a plain `||`, rather than one shared flag, since they're set from genuinely different call sites.
- **`isEditModeTransitioning`'s own "flip true" half is set during render, not inside an effect body** — React's own documented pattern for "adjust state when a value changes" (compare `editMode` against a previous value tracked in *state*, not a ref), required here because this project's own stricter lint config flags both an unconditional `setState` call inside an effect body (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) and a ref read/write during render (`react-hooks/refs`) — the state-comparison shape is the one that satisfies both simultaneously. `isTransitioningRef` (the ref twin used inside the async `measure()` callbacks, to avoid a stale-closure read the same way `editModeRef` already does for `editMode`) still gets its own write inside a `useLayoutEffect`, since ref writes belong in effects, not render.
- **`fixedOverheadPx`'s and `growableFixedOverheadPx`'s own `measure()` functions both add `if (isTransitioningRef.current) return;`** alongside their existing guards, freezing them for the transition's duration; a `setTimeout` (not `transitionend`) clears the flag and immediately calls both measure-refs once more to catch up, the same "re-measure once a gesture ends" pattern `handleResizeStart`'s own `handleEnd` already uses for an actual drag release.
- **`growableFixedOverheadPx` is also *pre-seeded* to its correct value the instant an `editMode` transition starts**, not merely left frozen at its stale prior value — confirmed live as a real, separate bug without this: on an editMode toggle, this value is exactly `dialogRef`'s own `marginBottom` (16px open, 0 closed) now that `growableRef` wraps `dialogRef` alone — a deterministic, known constant (`DIALOG_MARGIN_BOTTOM_PX`, matching the literal `var(--space-xl)` applied to the style, not independently measured), not something that actually needs a `ResizeObserver` round-trip to learn anymore. Leaving it frozen at browse-mode's stale value (0) under-compensated `marginTop` by 16px for the whole opening transition, reading as the slots/controls row visibly drifting before snapping to its correct position once the trailing catch-up measurement finally landed.

**The resize-arrow shortcuts gained the same animation, plus a new down arrow at 100%, in a follow-up once the core mechanism was confirmed stable.** The single-arrow-at-a-time cycle (see "The controls row" section above) is now a clean 3-state loop: below 50% of `poolPanelMaxHeight` shows the 50% arrow, 50–100% shows the 100% arrow, and at 100% shows a down arrow targeting `POOL_HEIGHT_MIN` (the default/minimum — matching the old, now-otherwise-removed Reset button's own target, not halfway back down to 50%) via a recreated `handleResetPoolHeight`. `ArrowDownIcon` (still exported from `icons.tsx`, just unused for a while) came back into `MixerBar.tsx`'s own imports for this.

**Known, accepted residual: the very first open of a fresh session shows a small (~4px), self-correcting settle blip** the slots/controls row wasn't previously visible taking, since it used to happen within an instant snap rather than a visible animation. Root cause: `fixedOverheadPx` bootstraps from `EDIT_MODE_FIXED_ROWS_HEIGHT` (a conservative worst-case constant) until its first real `ResizeObserver` measurement lands — already-documented, pre-existing behavior (see "A defensive guard for `fixedOverheadPx`/`growableFixedOverheadPx`" below) that this animation didn't introduce, just made marginally more visible. Confirmed live: the *second* open in the same session (once a real measurement has landed once) shows no such blip, deviation reduces to sub-pixel noise. Not fixed further — the bootstrap constant's own accepted tradeoff already covers this.

### The resize-drag's own `handleMove` had no `requestAnimationFrame` throttle at all — committed a real layout change on every raw `pointermove`

Reported live: dragging the resize grip visibly jitters/vibrates the controls row's own icons/labels, 100% of the time on mobile, intermittently on desktop — a *separate* report from the edit-panel open/close jitter (see the two sections above this one), and specifically about the live 1:1 drag, not the transitioned open/close/snap cases.

Measured concretely, not assumed: Chrome's own `Performance.getMetrics` CDP domain, sampled before/after a 30-step drag gesture, showed **29 real layout passes for 30 pointer-move events** — essentially one full reflow per raw input event. `handleMove` called `setDragHeightPx(next)` directly, synchronously, on every single `pointermove` it received, with no throttling of any kind. On a device or input whose pointer events fire faster than the display can actually paint (true of most touchscreens and higher-poll-rate mice), that's genuine, wasted reflow work spent on frames that could never even be shown — a plausible, and separately measurable, contributor to visible jank/jitter distinct from (and in addition to) the open/close-transition cost documented above.

**Fix**: `handleMove` no longer commits state directly. It computes the target height and stashes it in a plain closure variable (`pendingNext`), then schedules a single `requestAnimationFrame` callback (`flushPendingMove`) if one isn't already pending. Any number of `pointermove` events arriving between two paints only ever result in the *last* one being committed, once, right before the next frame. `ev.preventDefault()` stays outside the throttle, called synchronously on every raw event as before — it has to run within the event it's suppressing, deferring it a frame would be too late. `handleEnd` cancels any still-queued frame and flushes any pending value synchronously before reading `liveHeightRef` for `setPoolPanelHeightPref` — without this, the persisted height could be up to one frame stale relative to the pointer's true final position, and a queued frame firing *after* `setDragHeightPx(null)` would otherwise flicker the panel back to a mid-drag height for one frame right after release.

**Confirmed empirically, not just reasoned through**: a naive before/after comparison using Playwright's own paced `mouse.move()` calls showed no improvement in layout-pass count — traced this to Playwright's own dispatch pacing already landing close to one event per frame, which doesn't reproduce the actual "many raw events between two paints" scenario the fix targets. Rebuilt the test to dispatch a burst of raw `pointermove` events synchronously via `window.dispatchEvent` (guaranteed to land before the browser's next `requestAnimationFrame`, since rAF callbacks are always deferred to the *next* frame) and counted `MixerBar`'s own render count via a temporary `window.__mixerBarRenderCount` instrumentation, before and after letting one frame elapse. Result: bursts of 20, 100, and 400 synchronous events all produced exactly the same number of renders (4 — the fan-out from one `setDragHeightPx` call through this file's own pre-existing interdependent effects, unrelated to this fix) — a constant cost regardless of input volume, where the un-throttled code would have scaled linearly with event count. Also confirmed the final committed height still exactly matches the last real pointer position (a 120px drag reliably moved the grip 120px), ruling out the one-frame-stale risk `handleEnd`'s own flush-before-read guards against.

This fix is deliberately independent of, and lands before, a planned migration of this app's animation machinery to Motion (motion.dev) — it addresses the live-drag case specifically, which a FLIP-style/Motion `layout` animation can't help with at all (the real size must track the pointer exactly on every frame during an active drag; there's no discrete before/after state to interpolate between the way there is for the open/close/snap transitions).

### MixerBar's edit-panel open/close/resize migrated to a real hand-built FLIP, driven by Motion (motion/react) — step 3 (final) of the planned animation-library migration

The two sections above document the pre-Motion version of this box: `cardRef`'s `marginTop` and `dialogRef`'s `height`/`marginBottom` were CSS-`transition`ed directly, toggled on/off via `isEditModeTransitioning`/`isSnapTransitioning`. Chrome's own `Performance.getMetrics` (the same tool the `handleMove` fix above used) measured this at **29-35 real layout passes per open/close/resize interaction** — this is the specific measurement that originally motivated the whole Motion-migration plan (`/Users/sean/.claude/plans/deep-meandering-comet.md`), and this file was deliberately saved for last: "highest value, highest risk... a file with a track record of one subtle regression per touch."

**Why not Motion's `layout` prop** (the plan's own original proposal for this step): `layout` automates FLIP by having Motion measure an element's own rect before/after a re-render and animate the difference — but this file's `effectiveHeightPx`/`growableFixedOverheadPx`/`fixedOverheadPx` are already correct, synchronously-computed plain JS numbers, specifically engineered (see `growableFixedOverheadPx`'s own comment) to avoid a `ResizeObserver` sitting in the fast-changing (drag) path. Handing measurement over to `layout`'s own internal `ResizeObserver`-adjacent machinery would mean re-deriving a large fraction of this file's already-hard-won measurement logic against an unfamiliar, less controllable system, for a file with this exact regression history. Built a real, hand-driven FLIP instead, using Motion only for the actual animation *engine* (a compositor-only `transform`/`clip-path` overlay), while every existing measurement/state/guard mechanism stays untouched.

**The mechanism — a "committed vs. visual" split:**

- `targetMarginTop`/`targetHeight`/`targetMarginBottomPx` are exactly what the pre-Motion version computed and CSS-transitioned directly — unchanged.
- `committedMarginTop`/`committedHeight`/`committedMarginBottomPx` (new state) are what's actually applied as real, layout-affecting inline style on `cardRef`/`dialogRef`. Normally these mirror `target*` on every render, including every live-drag tick (drag is completely unaffected by any of this — see below) — **except** for the span of an active **shrink** transition, where committed deliberately lags at the pre-transition value.
- A **grow** (opening, or a resize-shortcut/drag increasing height) commits `committed* = target*` **instantly**, in the same render the transition is detected — the real layout settles in one atomic step, exactly like today's snap-shortcut jump used to, just now also true for open/close.
- A **shrink** (closing, or a decreasing resize-shortcut) leaves `committed*` untouched at the OLD value for the whole transition, and only catches up once the visual animation has actually finished (the same `setTimeout(EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS)` that already cleared `isEditModeTransitioning`/`isSnapTransitioning` pre-migration now also does this).
- Direction (`transitionDirection: "grow" | "shrink" | null`) is decided once, at the exact instant a transition starts, by comparing the new target against whichever height is currently `committed` (not `target`, which could already equal it) — at the three call sites: the pre-existing `editMode`-toggle render-phase block, `handleSnapPoolHeight`, `handleResetPoolHeight`.
- A visual overlay — `growableRef`'s `transform: translateY(...)`, `dialogRef`'s `clip-path: inset(0 0 Npx 0)` — bridges whatever gap exists between the real, committed geometry and what the user should perceive at each instant, animated by Motion's imperative `animate()` (targeting the plain DOM refs directly — `growableRef`/`dialogRef` stay ordinary `<div>`s, not `motion.div`, since `animate()` works on any element). The formula (`offset = visualTarget(t) − committedNow`) is direction-agnostic: plugging in "whichever of start/end `committed` currently equals" produces the correct from/to pair for *either* direction without the animation code needing to know which one is in flight. (First shipped targeting `cardRef` itself — confirmed live as a real bug and fixed; see the dedicated entry below for why `growableRef` is the correct target.)

**Why this reduces the layout-pass count**: `marginTop`/`height`/`margin-bottom` — genuine layout properties — now only ever change in one synchronous step per transition (immediately for a grow, once at the very end for a shrink), never smoothly interpolated by the browser frame-by-frame the way a CSS `transition` on them does. `transform` and `clip-path` are the two properties actually animating across the visible 280ms window, and both are compositor-only. Confirmed via the same `Performance.getMetrics` methodology used above: open and close transitions now cost **2-4 real layout passes total**, not per frame — roughly a 10-15x reduction from the pre-migration 29-35.

**A subtle bug found and fixed during verification, not assumed away**: Motion's `animate()` uses the Web Animations API under the hood, and an animation that has reached its own natural end keeps applying its last frame indefinitely (a "finished, fill-forward" effect) — this outranks a later, independent `element.style.transform = ""` write, which is NOT sufficient on its own to clear it. Confirmed live via a `getComputedStyle` sampler: the overlay stayed stuck at its final in-transition value (e.g. a `clip-path` clipped to the pre-shrink amount) indefinitely after a transition should have visually finished. `AnimationPlaybackControls.stop()` (Motion's own wrapper) reliably clears this for an animation that's still actively running, but turned out to be a no-op once the animation had already reached its natural end on its own — which, by the time this file's own settle timer fires (deliberately matching `EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS`, the same duration the animation itself runs for), it always has. The actual fix: `element.getAnimations().forEach(a => a.cancel())` — the native Web Animations API's own `.cancel()`, not Motion's `.stop()` — which unconditionally removes an animation's effect regardless of its current `playState`, letting the plain inline-style reset that follows immediately take visible effect either way.

**What stayed exactly as it was, and why (per this migration's own removal-discipline rule — nothing gets deleted without live re-verification that Motion genuinely subsumes it):**

- **`isTransitioningRef`/`isEditModeTransitioning`/`isSnapTransitioning`** (the `ResizeObserver`-freeze mechanism) — **kept, not removed**. The plan's own original hope was that these might become redundant once Motion "handles the layout diffing" — but this migration's design still has a genuine multi-frame window where the real DOM hasn't reached its final geometry yet (the whole span of a deferred shrink), so measuring `fixedOverheadPx`/`growableFixedOverheadPx` mid-transition would still read a wrong, stale value exactly as before. The race these flags guard against is fully present under the new mechanism too, just for a documented, different underlying reason (deliberate deferral, not CSS interpolation) — so per this migration's own binding rule, they were verified to still be load-bearing, not assumed obsolete.
- **`pinScrollToDocumentBottomIfWasAtBottom`** — untouched, and re-verified live (scroll to true document bottom, then open edit mode): still correctly keeps the page pinned near the bottom. This mechanism operates on real, already-committed scroll/layout state and never depended on *how* `marginTop` reached its value.
- **Live drag (`handleMove`/`handleResizeStart`/`handleEnd`, and the RAF-throttle fix documented above)** — completely unaffected. Drag never sets `isEditModeTransitioning`/`isSnapTransitioning`, so it never engages any part of this migration's committed/visual-overlay machinery; `committed*` just tracks `target*` directly, 1:1, every drag tick, exactly as it did pre-migration. Confirmed live: a 120px drag still moves the panel exactly 120px, with zero transform/clip-path applied throughout.
- **`frozenReachability`, the click-outside allowlist, wheel/touch-blocking, focus management** — none of these are about how the geometry animates; all untouched.

**Verified, not assumed** (Playwright, this file's own established methodology): open/close/snap-50%/snap-100%/reset-to-min all settle at the correct final geometry with the overlay cleanly reset to identity afterward; a live drag tracks the pointer 1:1 with no overlay ever applied; an *interrupted* transition (clicking 50% then immediately 100% before the first snap's own 280ms elapses) settles cleanly at the second target, no stuck state; a rapid 10x open/close "mash" (the same stress-test class that found the original `ResizeObserver`/compositor-desync bugs this file has already fixed once) settles cleanly and leaves the panel fully interactive afterward; mobile-width rendering (grip present, 50%/100%/reset shortcuts correctly hidden per `canShowResizeHandleRow`'s own `min-[993px]` gate) behaves identically. Layout-pass counts and settle-correctness are both headless-verifiable; the *feel* of the animation (60fps smoothness on a real device) is not — see the dedicated entry immediately below for the real, on-device-only bug this gap in headless coverage let through on the first pass.

### The first version of the above shipped a real regression: the fixed slots/controls row visibly moved on every open/close/resize, plus a return of the original jitter — found on-device, not in headless testing

Reported live, on both desktop and mobile, immediately after the FLIP migration above first shipped: opening, closing, or resizing the pool panel visibly moved the slots/controls row itself ("the bottom mix bar") down and back, with a distinct "graphical glitch of the panel and mixbar moving apart and coming back together" — plus a return of this session's very first reported symptom, jitter/vibration in the controls row's own icons/buttons/labels during the same interactions.

**Root cause, found by re-examining the DOM structure, not by guessing**: `cardRef` is the ancestor of *both* `growableRef` (the pool panel) *and* the fixed "Row 2" slots/controls row — they're siblings, both direct children of `cardRef`. The FLIP overlay's compositor-only `transform` was applied to `cardRef` itself, on the reasoning that it needed to visually cancel `cardRef`'s own `marginTop` jump.
