That reasoning had a hole: a `transform` on `cardRef` doesn't just move the pool panel visually — it moves *everything inside `cardRef`*, including the "Row 2" sibling, which this file's entire design exists specifically to keep from ever moving. Since the real, `committed` `marginTop`/`height` were *already* correct (a grow commits instantly; a shrink's real values don't change until the transition ends, at which point the overlay is already reset to identity), the slots/controls row's real position was correct throughout — but the overlay's fake, animated offset dragged it away from that correct position and back, for the whole 280ms of every transition. That drag-and-return is exactly the reported "moving apart and coming back together" glitch, and the same per-frame sub-pixel transform interpolation applied to the controls row's own text/icons is a very plausible explanation for the jitter's return too (the identical family of symptom this session's very first investigation traced to sub-pixel-affecting properties changing on the controls row, just via a different mechanism this time).

**Fix**: retarget the `transform` animation from `cardRef` to `growableRef` — the pool panel's own wrapper, a sibling of "Row 2," not its ancestor. `growableRef`'s on-screen position moves by exactly the same amount `cardRef`'s does (nothing sits between them but `cardRef`'s own border/padding, which don't change), so the overlay still correctly cancels the same visual jump for the pool panel itself — but a transform confined to `growableRef` cannot affect a sibling element under CSS's own box model, so "Row 2" is now structurally unable to move as a side effect of this animation, rather than merely *happening* not to move under a particular set of values.

**Verified directly, not just re-reasoned**: sampled the slots/controls row's own `getBoundingClientRect().top` every ~20ms throughout an open, a close, a 50% resize-shortcut snap, and a full manual drag-resize — `top` reads bit-for-bit identical (max delta 0.00px) across every sample, every scenario. This is a stronger check than the original "did it settle at the right final value" verification: it confirms the row *never* moves, not just that it ends up back where it started.

### Two more real bugs, found the same on-device retest round: a render-doubling drag jitter, and a genuine one-frame race on open

**Bug 1 — drag jitter, traced to a real design flaw found on code review, not assumed fixed by the `growableRef` retarget above.** `committedMarginTop`/`committedHeight`/`committedMarginBottomPx` were their own persisted React state, synced to `target*` via an unconditional `setCommitted*(...)` call inside the render body (a render-phase conditional-setState, this file's own established pattern for "adjust state when a value changes") — correct in *outcome*, but this pattern has a real cost React itself documents: any `setState` call during render forces React to immediately re-invoke the whole component function a second time before committing. That's cheap and appropriate for the three real transition triggers (an `editMode` toggle, a snap-shortcut click) — genuinely rare events — but the same block ran on **every drag tick** too, since dragging changes `effectiveHeightPx`/`target*` on every `pointermove`. Every single drag frame was silently costing a full extra React render pass it didn't need, on top of the drag's own already-necessary one.

**Fix**: removed `committedMarginTop`/`committedHeight`/`committedMarginBottomPx` as persisted state entirely. What's actually applied to `cardRef`/`dialogRef` is now a **pure computation** every render — `isShowingPreTransitionGeometry ? transitionStartX : targetX` — with zero `setState` calls involved for the idle/growing/dragging cases (`isShowingPreTransitionGeometry` is only ever true during an active shrink's own deferred-commit window). `transitionStartMarginTop`/`transitionStartHeight`/`transitionStartMarginBottomPx` (already-existing state, previously used only to feed the animation's own from/to keyframes) now double as the one thing that still needs capturing at the three real transition-start call sites — computed there from `prevEditModeForTransition` (the editMode branch) or read directly from `target*` at invocation time (the two event-handler-driven snap functions, which have no same-render staleness to work around at all). A drag tick now costs exactly what it cost before this whole migration: one render, no more.

**Bug 2 — a genuine ~1-frame race between the real commit and the overlay's own first-frame paint, found via direct frame sampling, not guessed at.** Reported live as "opening the panel makes the whole mixbar jut upward" and "the panel lingers behind longer than it should." Built a `requestAnimationFrame`-driven sampler logging `growableRef`'s computed `transform`, `dialogRef`'s computed `clip-path`, and both elements' real geometry, every frame across an open. Result: the frame where `cardMarginTop`/`dialogHeight` first landed on their real, fully-grown final values showed `growableTransform: none` / `dialogClip: none` — the compensating overlay hadn't been painted yet — and only the *next* frame (~4-16ms later) actually showed the overlay's own starting offset. For that one frame, the browser painted the fully-grown real geometry with **no** compensation at all: a raw, unmasked jump to the final state, immediately followed by a visible jerk back to the overlay's starting position once it finally caught up — exactly the reported "jut upward, then linger" quality of motion. Root cause: calling Motion's `animate()` synchronously inside `useLayoutEffect` (itself synchronous, before paint) does not guarantee the underlying Web Animations API animation paints its own first keyframe in that same frame — evidently there's a scheduling gap between creating a WAAPI animation and it actually being reflected in the very next paint.

**Fix**: set `growable.style.transform`/`dialog.style.clipPath` to their own `from` values **directly, synchronously**, immediately before calling `animate()` — not relying on `animate()`'s own keyframe-array `[from, to]` to apply `from` in time. Re-ran the same frame sampler after the fix: the first frame showing the real, grown geometry now *also* shows the overlay already at its correct starting offset — zero gap, confirmed directly, not inferred.

**Separately investigated and found to be pre-existing, not a regression from this migration**: the resize-shortcut arrow (50%/100%/reset) visibly swaps to its next icon the instant a click lands, while the panel itself still takes ~280ms to visually catch up — reported alongside the two bugs above as "the delay of affordances changing once clicked is off for resize." Checked git history: `isAtFullHeight`/`isBelowHalfHeight` (which choose the arrow) have always been computed directly from `effectiveHeightPx`, live and instant, independent of whatever mechanism animates the panel's own visual resize. This exact mismatch was introduced when the pool panel's open/close animation was first added at all (a CSS-transition-based version, well before this session's Motion migration began) — not something either the CSS-transition version or this Motion/FLIP version newly broke. Flagged, not fixed here — a real UX inconsistency worth addressing on its own terms (e.g. deferring the arrow's own icon swap to track the visual completion instead of the click), but out of scope for "regressions this migration introduced."

### Replaced the hand-built FLIP above with Motion's own `layout` prop — after the growableRef fix, drag-jitter fix, and frame-race fix, the panel was still glitchy on both desktop and mobile

Reported live, in the same round as the fixes above (still not resolved by them): jitter while resizing, the panel "springing up instantly" on open with content filling in slowly after (not a smooth grow), the same instant-spring-then-catch-up on the resize up-arrow, a broken-looking downward animation on the resize down-arrow, and the fixed slots/controls row still springing on a reopen after a full open/close cycle. Asked directly to reassess against Motion's own current documentation before patching further: is a hand-tracked "committed vs. visual" split with an imperative `animate()` call genuinely the right tool, or is this exactly the case Motion's `layout` prop (automatic FLIP, driven by measuring an element's own rendered rect before/after a commit) exists for?

**Checked motion.dev directly rather than relying on training data.** Confirmed: `layout` (or `layout="position"`/`layout="size"` for narrower cases) automatically detects a layout change on every render and animates it via `transform` (translate + scale), with the `transition` prop configuring duration/easing per element. Also confirmed Motion's own documented fix for the "scaling a parent visually distorts its children" problem: give the child `layout` too, and Motion counter-scales it so it renders undistorted — the same underlying mechanism this file's original hand-built version was trying to replicate by hand with `translateY`/`clip-path`.

**Replaced the entire hand-built FLIP** (`committedMarginTop`/`committedHeight`/`committedMarginBottomPx`, `transitionStartMarginTop`/`EndMarginTop`/`StartHeight`/`EndHeight`/`StartMarginBottomPx`, `transitionDirection`, `transitionGen`, `isShowingPreTransitionGeometry`, `renderedMarginTop`/`Height`/`MarginBottomPx`, and the imperative `animate()`-driven `useLayoutEffect` with its settle timeout and cleanup — roughly 150 lines) with `layout` on the actual DOM elements. `targetMarginTop`/`targetHeight`/`targetMarginBottomPx` are now applied directly, every render, with no "committed vs. target" distinction and no deferred-commit timing at all — Motion's `layout` prop is what makes a real, instant, one-step jump (in *either* direction, symmetric — unlike the old version's shrink-specific deferred commit) look smooth.

**Getting the topology right took three more empirically-found, not guessed-at, corrections:**

1. **`dialogRef` does not get its own `layout` prop, despite Motion's own "give the child `layout` too" distortion guidance — that guidance is written for a different shape of problem than this one.** Motion's own docs example (`<motion.section layout><motion.img layout /></motion.section>`) is for a child whose *own* intrinsic size stays constant, only visually distorted by an ancestor's scale. Here, `growableRef` (the direct, zero-padding parent of `dialogRef` — its rect *is* `dialogRef`'s rect) wraps `dialogRef` alone, and `dialogRef`'s own `height` is the actual property changing every render. Giving `dialogRef` `layout` too meant it was independently re-detecting and re-FLIP-animating the *same* size delta `growableRef` was already animating — confirmed via a `getBoundingClientRect()` frame sampler: with `layout` on both, `dialogRef`'s own measured height crept up gradually, frame by frame (16px, 21px, 26px...) instead of reading its real, already-final value immediately from the first sampled frame. Removed `layout` (and the `motion.div`, since a plain `div` with no motion props behaves identically) from `dialogRef` entirely. Per the user's own explicit direction after seeing this — "remove the animation for the content inside and just focus on the panel animation for now, later we can better tweak the inside content animation" — `TagPoolBody`'s own content now simply rides along with `growableRef`'s single outer scale, with no independent animation or distortion-correction of its own; some transient, brief visual scaling of the content during the ~280ms transition is an accepted tradeoff for this round, not a further-chased bug.

2. **`layout` must be fully *disabled*, not just given a `duration: 0` transition, for the span of a live drag.** Motion's own docs describe `layout` as "drag-compatible by default," but that's specifically about Motion's *own* `drag` gesture prop — this file's grip drag is a plain, manually-tracked pointer gesture (`handleMove`/`handleResizeStart`, RAF-throttled to one commit per frame) driving an ordinary style change, not Motion's own drag system, and empirically the two don't behave the same. Confirmed live: with `layout` left enabled and only `transition={{ duration: 0 }}` during a drag, the real underlying value tracked the pointer correctly (the panel always settled at exactly the right height on release), but the *painted* height visibly lagged far behind the pointer throughout the drag itself — measured directly: dragging the grip 150px only visibly grew the panel from 160px to ~165px until release, at which point it snapped straight to the correct ~310px. Motion's own layout-measurement/projection cycle, even with zero animation duration, still runs asynchronously relative to React's render, and couldn't keep up with a real gesture committing a new value on every animation frame. Fixed with `layout={dragHeightPx === null}` (a genuinely conditional prop, not a transition tweak) on every layout-animated element in this file — `layout` re-enables itself the instant the drag ends, ready for whatever comes next. Re-verified after the fix: a 150px drag now reads 210px (at the 50px mark) and 310px (at 150px) at every sampled checkpoint, with zero lag.

3. **`cardRef` itself needs `layout` too — not just `growableRef` — with the fixed "Row 2" slots/controls row getting `layout` as well, purely as Motion's own counter-correction.** The version that only put `layout` on `growableRef` (reasoning: `cardRef` is the ancestor of both `growableRef` and the fixed Row 2, so a transform on `cardRef` itself would drag Row 2 along with it — the exact bug documented above) was correct as far as it went, but incomplete: `cardRef`'s own border — this app's single most visually prominent cue for "is the panel growing" — never visibly animated at all, since `cardRef`'s own `marginTop` was applied as a plain, instant, un-animated style. Only `growableRef`'s own *inner* content area animated (a subtle scale, easy to miss against a border that had already snapped to its final size). Reported live as "the panel itself is not seemingly animated, just appears on resizes... only the manual resize animates appropriately" — drag reads as smooth purely because it updates `marginTop` directly, on every frame, matching the pointer, not because anything is actually animating. Fixed by giving `layout` to `cardRef` too (so its own border now visibly, smoothly rises and falls) *and* to the Row 2 wrapper — Motion's own documented distortion-fix mechanism, just applied to a position-only case rather than the size/image case the docs illustrate: Row 2's own `layout` prop measures its true, pre-transform rect (which never actually changes) and applies the exact inverse of whatever transform `cardRef` now carries, keeping it visually stationary despite being `cardRef`'s own child. Verified directly, not assumed: the same `getBoundingClientRect()` sampler already used throughout this migration shows 0.00-0.75px drift (sub-pixel, float-rounding noise) across every transition — open, close, both resize-shortcut directions, a live drag, and a reopen after a full open/close cycle — with the border now visibly growing progressively across a sequence of screenshots (a small, still-growing panel at 40ms into the transition; nearly complete at 140ms) rather than snapping instantly.

**Net effect (at the time this was written)**: three `motion.div` elements carried `layout={dragHeightPx === null}` + a shared `transition={layoutTransition}` object (`cardRef`, `growableRef`, the Row 2 wrapper) — `dialogRef` stayed a plain, unanimated `div`. See the entry immediately below for why point 3 above was itself reverted shortly after.

### Point 3 above (giving `cardRef` and the Row 2 wrapper their own `layout`) was itself reverted — two independently-computed Motion layout animations trying to cancel out exactly, every frame, is fragile in practice, not just in theory

Reported live, immediately after the version described above shipped: visible vibration/pulsing in Row 2's own content (the "add tag" slot borders changing size, control icons vibrating), and the outer card border/shadow "animating a lot" on open/close rather than moving as one clean, stable unit. Requested directly and unambiguously: the "base mixbar" (this card's own border/shadow, and every control in Row 2) must never carry *any* animation of its own unless it's a specifically-designed effect — only the pool panel itself should visibly animate on open/close/resize.

**Root cause**: `cardRef`'s own `layout`-driven transform and the Row 2 wrapper's own counter-correcting `layout` are two *separate* Motion animation instances, each independently measuring and interpolating on its own schedule. Sharing the same `transition={layoutTransition}` config object guarantees they're configured identically, but does not guarantee bit-for-bit identical interpolated output at every sampled frame — and Row 2's content (small icons, thin borders, tight text) is exactly the kind of rendering that makes even a sub-pixel mismatch between "cardRef's real transform" and "Row 2's attempted cancellation" visible as shimmer/vibration. This is a structurally different, and more fragile, situation than `growableRef`/`dialogRef`'s own relationship (a single `layout` animation, with `dialogRef` deliberately *not* getting its own competing one) or than a single element's `layout` prop generally.

**Fix**: reverted `cardRef` and the Row 2 wrapper both back to plain, unanimated `div`s — exactly the topology from the "getting the topology right" entry's own point 1/starting position, with only `growableRef` carrying `layout`. `cardRef`'s own border/shadow, and everything in Row 2, are now applied as plain, instant, real style values with zero Motion involvement of any kind — matching the explicit request that nothing in this file should animate unless it's a specifically-designed effect. This does mean `cardRef`'s own border no longer visibly, smoothly rises/falls the way point 3 above achieved — only the panel's own inner reveal (`growableRef`'s scale) animates, with the outer frame's real size simply snapping to match on each commit. Re-verified via the same `getBoundingClientRect()` sampler: the "add tag" slot's own position now reads bit-for-bit identical (`top: 724.42`) across closed, mid-open, settled, and re-closed states, with zero drift at any sampled point.

**Historical note, checked directly rather than relying on memory**: asked whether the pre-Motion CSS-transition version of this panel (`cb72324`, the commit that first added `transitionProperty: "margin-top"` / `"height, margin-bottom"` directly to `cardRef`/`dialogRef`, no FLIP of any kind) was genuinely jitter-free. Confirmed via `git show`: that commit already had the *exact same* `isEditModeTransitioning`/`isSnapTransitioning` gating this file still uses, applied as a single, plain CSS transition on two properties, on `cardRef`/`dialogRef` directly — no split into `growableRef`/Row 2 at all, no FLIP overlay. This is structurally the simplest possible version of this animation, and per the report, it worked cleanly at the time. Checked out that exact commit (`8d82c5c`, two commits before the RollingLabel/card Motion migration steps, with the resize-drag RAF-throttle fix already landed but `MixerBar.tsx`'s own animation mechanism still completely unchanged from `cb72324`) in an isolated worktree and re-measured directly: the fixed row's own position was already stable there too (sub-pixel noise only), consistent with a *plain* CSS transition on `marginTop`/`height` never having been the source of visible row movement — the row-movement bugs this session hit and fixed were specific to this file's own later, more elaborate FLIP-overlay attempts, not inherent to animating this panel at all. The `Performance.getMetrics` layout-pass cost (29-35 per interaction, unrelated to row movement) was very likely already present at that commit too, just not yet perceptible as jitter on the hardware being tested on at the time — this migration's whole premise (get that cost down to 2-6 passes) still stands regardless.

**Correction, from a later session round: `8d82c5c` is not a safe stand-in for "pre-Motion" and shouldn't be cited as one.** It sits *after* the resize-drag RAF-throttle fix (noted above, correctly, as already landed by that point) — a real behavioral change made in anticipation of this migration, not part of the original, untouched pre-Motion code. Flagged directly: "8282c5c is not far back enough... you need to go to at least [`7b8fe1bf0053720fcfcdd49695ab2caa81d57711`]." Confirmed directly this round: at `7b8fe1b` (checked out in an isolated worktree, `npm install`ed and run standalone — confirmed via `grep` that neither `motion/react` nor `framer-motion` appears anywhere in the tree at that commit, genuinely pre-Motion, not just pre-this-file's-own-migration-step), the same `Performance.getMetrics` methodology measured **34 layout passes for open, 35 for close** — closely matching the existing 29-35 figure above, so the number itself holds up, but `7b8fe1b` (and earlier) is now the correct commit to cite or re-check against for any future "what did this cost before Motion" comparison, not `8d82c5c`.

### The animation moved from `growableRef` to `cardRef`+Row 2, using `layout="position"` instead of full `layout` — the fix that finally let the border animate without distorting anything

Reported live after the `growableRef`-only version above shipped: "whatever you changed broke the panel animation and returned the tabs and tags inside the panel animating... we want the border of the panel to animate smoothly as it opens, closes, or resizes not the content inside of it." Root cause was structural, not a tuning miss: `growableRef`'s own `layout` prop fakes its size change via a *scale* transform scoped to `growableRef`'s own box — that box sits entirely *inside* `cardRef`'s already-final, un-animated border, so the scale was necessarily invisible on the border itself (which was already at its correct final rect from frame 1, since `cardRef`'s real `marginTop`/height are never gradually interpolated, only snapped) while visibly stretching `TagPoolBody`'s own tabs/chips, which sit directly inside the scaled box. No amount of retuning `growableRef` alone could ever make the *border* animate — the fake transform has to be applied to an element whose rendered box actually includes the border, i.e. `cardRef` itself.

**Why not just give `cardRef` full `layout` again** (the thing already tried and reverted for causing Row 2 vibration, above)? Full `layout` fakes a size change via *scale*, and `cardRef` contains Row 2 as a real child — scaling `cardRef` visibly stretches/squishes Row 2 (thinner or thicker borders, distorted icons) unless Row 2 gets an exactly-matching counter-scale, which is the two-independently-computed-animations fragility already documented above. **`layout="position"`** (confirmed against motion.dev directly, not assumed) animates *only* an element's position via `translate` — size changes snap instantly, and translate never distorts a child's own rendered dimensions the way scale does. Given `cardRef`'s real geometry is already snap-instant by design, and `layout="position"`'s whole contract is "let the size snap, animate the position," this is a precise match rather than a workaround.

**Final topology**: `cardRef` gets `layout={dragHeightPx === null ? "position" : false}` (disabled outright during a live drag, same reasoning as every earlier `layout` prop in this file — Motion's measurement cycle can't track a per-frame pointer gesture). Row 2 gets the *same* `layout="position"` prop, with the *same* `transition={layoutTransition}` object. `growableRef` and `dialogRef` are both back to plain, fully inert `div`s — no `layout`, no motion props at all; `TagPoolBody`'s tabs/chips are simply present at their real, final layout from the first frame of every transition, matching the user's own explicit "remove the animation for the content inside... focus on the panel animation for now."

**Why Row 2 needs its own `layout="position"` too, not just a passive inherit of `cardRef`'s transform**: `cardRef`'s own translate necessarily also visually carries Row 2 along (transforms apply to the whole rendered subtree, not just the element they're declared on) — but Row 2's *own* rect, measured before vs. after any transition, is *always* identical by construction (that's the entire point of `targetMarginTop`'s negative-margin math: Row 2's absolute position never actually changes, open or closed). Giving Row 2 its own `layout="position"` node lets Motion's own projection system compute that zero-delta directly and counter it exactly, rather than Row 2 passively riding whatever `cardRef`'s own in-flight transform happens to be at each instant — confirmed live via a `getBoundingClientRect()` frame sampler that the *passive* version visibly displaces Row 2 by the full open/close delta for one frame (jumping to `final + delta` immediately, then easing back to `final`) before this fix, vs. **0.00px drift at every single sampled frame**, all scenarios, after it. Unlike the earlier cardRef/Row-2 pairing that vibrated, this one is robust rather than merely "shares a config object": `layout="position"` never scales anything for either element, so there's no float-precision race between two independently-computed *scale* factors to go wrong — Row 2 either has literally nothing to animate (the common case) or a clean, single translate, with no counter-scale math involved anywhere.

**Two further bugs found and fixed while chasing a large-jump edge case** (reopening the panel directly at a big, *persisted* height — e.g. snap to 100%, close, reopen — from a page scrolled to its true bottom), both confirmed via direct `getBoundingClientRect()`/`window.scrollY` instrumentation, not guessed at:

1. **A render-phase `setState` pre-seed left `targetMarginTop` genuinely different across two consecutive render invocations for one editMode toggle.** `growableFixedOverheadPx` is deliberately pre-seeded via a conditional `setState` call in the render body the instant `editMode` changes (React's documented "adjust state when a prop changes" pattern), specifically so it isn't left stale for a whole transition. That pre-seed's *effect* on `targetMarginTop`, however, only lands on React's own synchronous re-invocation of the component function — normally invisible (React documents this as never actually painting the intermediate pass) — but Motion's `layout` measures on every render *invocation*, not once per commit, and was confirmed live to animate toward that first, stale, later-discarded value (`-590`) before snapping to and continuing from the corrected one (`-606`) a render later. Fixed by computing the same pre-seed value inline (`effectiveGrowableFixedOverheadPx`, gated on a new `editModeJustToggled` flag) so `targetMarginTop` is already correct on the very first render of a toggle, rather than depending on a second pass to get there — the `setState` call itself stays, for every *later* render's benefit.
2. **The scroll-pin loop (`pinScrollToDocumentBottomIfWasAtBottom`) fighting Motion for the same sticky element's measured position.** `cardRef` is `position: sticky`, whose `getBoundingClientRect()` — what `layout="position"` reads every render to compute its transform — is directly a function of the live scroll offset. This file's own scroll-pin mechanism (unrelated to Motion, pre-existing) calls `window.scrollTo` repeatedly while `wasAtDocumentBottomRef` is true, specifically so the page doesn't visibly jump as its real height changes. Calling that *while* Motion is mid-transition on `cardRef` knocks its measured rect off by roughly the scrolled amount: live-measured, `cardRef`'s own top plateaued at 313px (706px start minus the pin's own ~393px scroll jump) for ~170ms before continuing to its real ~100px target, rather than interpolating smoothly the whole way — confirmed to be `cardRef` alone doing this (not a Row-2-desync) by disabling Row 2's own `layout` prop entirely and re-measuring: `cardRef` showed the identical plateau with no other element involved. Fixed by deferring the toggle-triggered pin call (not the drag ones, which legitimately need to track a moving real value every frame) until `EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS` after the toggle, once Motion's own transform has fully settled back to identity — the page's real `scrollHeight` is already at its final value from the same commit the toggle itself lands in, so there's no correctness reason to fight Motion for it mid-flight.

**Verified, not assumed**: the full existing scratchpad verification suite (open, close, both resize-shortcut directions, a live drag, a rapid open/close-mashing stress test, a reopen-after-full-open/close cycle, and the newly-added large-persisted-height reopen case) all show **0.00px** drift on Row 2/the tag slots at every sampled frame, `cardRef`'s own `transform` computed style is a pure `matrix(1, 0, 0, 1, 0, Y)` (translate-only, confirmed zero scale/skew component) throughout every transition and resets to `none` once settled, drag remains 1:1 responsive, and the CDP `Performance.getMetrics` layout-pass count stays at 3 per transition (vs. the pre-Motion 29-35 baseline).

### Row 2's controls didn't actually all share one height — `ActionButton`'s `md` size was padding-derived, not fixed, and drifted by ±0.5px depending on which glyphs a given button's own label happened to contain

Reported live: "the buttons in the control area of the mixbar have some differing heights." Measured directly with `getBoundingClientRect()`: `TagSlot`/`IconButton size="md"` were a rock-solid `44.5px` (both already used a hardcoded height), but `ActionButton`'s `md` size — Tag Pool/Done ✓, Clear, Random, RECOMMEND — used `px-[var(--space-xl)] py-[var(--space-lg)]` with no explicit height at all, so its real rendered height was whatever the button's own line-box (padding + font ascent/descent for that specific label's glyphs) happened to compute to. "RECOMMEND ▸"/"Done ✓"/"Tag Pool" measured `44.5px`; "Clear ✕"/"Random ×3" measured `45px` — a real, confirmed half-pixel drift traced to the `✕`/`×` glyphs having very slightly different font metrics than the rest, in a control whose height was never actually pinned to a number. Fixed the same way `ActionButton`'s own `sm` size already had to be, for the identical reason (see that size's own comment): `md` is now `inline-flex h-[44.5px] items-center justify-center px-[var(--space-xl)]`, matching `TagSlot`/`IconButton` exactly regardless of label content. `MatchSelector`'s segmented control (`32.5px`, padding-derived) is deliberately *not* included in this — confirmed directly with the user that it's fine being shorter, unlike the rest of the row.

Also fixed in the same pass: `TagPoolIcon` (`16×16`) and `ArrowRightFilledIcon` (RECOMMEND's own collapsed-icon form, `15×15`) were rendering smaller than `CheckIcon`/`EraserIcon`/`DiceIcon` (all `17×17`) despite sitting in identically-sized `44.5px` icon buttons — both share the same `viewBox="0 0 24 24"` as the rest, so bumping their `width`/`height` attributes to `17` is a pure proportional scale-up, no path changes. Re-verified live at a narrow viewport (icon-only breakpoint): all three of Tag Pool/Clear/Random now measure `44.5×44.5` box / `17×17` icon, byte-identical.

### RECOMMEND's own icon-only form still didn't match — it's one `ActionButton` with conditional content, not two sibling instances like its three neighbors, so it needed a different fix

Requested directly, as a follow-up once the three above matched each other: RECOMMEND's collapsed icon sat inside `px-[var(--space-xl)]` horizontal padding (ActionButton's own unconditional `md`-size padding) rather than the tight, centered `44.5×44.5` square the other three use. Switching RECOMMEND to a literal `IconButton` (matching its neighbors structurally) was considered and rejected: `IconButton`'s own props deliberately omit `disabled` entirely (see that component's own comment — a native `disabled` blocks clicks outright, which `visuallyDisabled` alone doesn't), and RECOMMEND's real `disabled={!enabled}` plus its `accent` color-swap both depend on `ActionButton`'s own handling of that attribute — reproducing both on `IconButton` would mean duplicating logic that already exists, for a component whose props actively fight the use case. Fixed narrower instead: `max-[1070px]:w-[44.5px] max-[1070px]:px-0` overrides just the icon-only breakpoint's width and horizontal padding, letting the same `items-center justify-center` `SIZE_CLASSES.md` already provides center the icon with zero padding in a forced-square box — safe against Tailwind's own "unprefixed utilities aren't ordered by source position" trap (see `IconButton.tsx`'s `display` prop comment) specifically because these are `max-width`-*variant* utilities, not two bare unprefixed ones on the same property; variants are always layered after their bare counterparts in Tailwind's generated CSS regardless of source order. Verified live: all four of Tag Pool/Clear/Random/Recommend now measure `44.5×44.5` box / `17×17` icon / `13.75px` effective padding on each side, byte-identical.

### Exhaustive audit, requested directly, found zero measurable layout drift anywhere in this bar — every padding/icon/button/container dimension was checked across desktop and mobile viewports, every responsive breakpoint, live drag, resize snaps, page scroll, and panel-internal scroll

Two separate reports prompted this: MIN MATCH's own dimensions possibly drifting on scroll, and a suspicion that something in the row was shifting during a manual panel resize specifically (distinct from the scroll-through bug below). Checked directly rather than assumed, via `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` snapshots at every frame of: a live 150px drag (sampled continuously, both at a desktop width showing full-text buttons and a narrow width showing icon-only forms), a 50%/100% resize-shortcut snap (30 frames, `requestAnimationFrame`-sampled), page `scrollTo`/wheel scrolling (both with the panel closed and open, at a viewport genuinely taller than its content so scrolling has somewhere to go), wheel-scrolling directly over the chip grid, and a CDP-synthetic touch-drag gesture over the chip grid (`Input.dispatchTouchEvent`, an iPhone 13 emulation profile). Tracked: Row 2's own rect, every tag slot, Clear/Random/Tag Pool/Recommend in both their text and icon forms, MIN MATCH's segmented control, every SVG icon's own rendered size, and `cardEl`/`dialogRef`/`growableRef`'s own padding/margin computed styles.

**Result: zero drift, at any sampled frame, in any of it** — every dimension read back byte-for-byte identical across every scenario tested. This rules out a React-state/CSS-layout bug as the cause of either report with fairly high confidence — if MIN MATCH or any control's own box were genuinely recomputing to a different value mid-interaction, this sampling approach (the same one that already caught the real `cardRef`/Row 2 desync bugs earlier in this file) would have shown it.

Given that, both the scroll-through report below and a resize-time "jitter" report are very likely **rendering/compositing-level phenomena** — real on a physical device, but invisible to `getBoundingClientRect()`, which only reports the logical end-state of a frame, not whether the browser's own paint/composite step was smooth getting there. The scroll-through fix below (splitting `position: sticky` off the element Motion applies a `transform` to) is the leading, directly-actionable hypothesis for both reports, not just the scroll-through one, since a known WebKit compositor quirk in that exact combination could plausibly explain jank around resize/move as well as leaked scroll gestures — flagged here in case on-device testing shows it helps (or doesn't) with the resize jitter specifically, which would be useful signal either way.

### Scroll-through-to-the-page bug is back, on mobile — `position: sticky` and Motion's own `transform` ended up on the same element, a known category of WebKit interaction quirk

Reported live, with a pointer to this exact bug's own pre-Motion history (`blockScrollThrough`'s dialog-exclusion fix, `data-scroll-fade-orientation`, the `touch-action` compositor-recognition entries above): scrolling a gesture that starts on the open panel leaks through and scrolls the whole page behind it again, confirmed by the user to reproduce on mobile specifically. **Not reproducible here** — every angle attempted in headless Chromium still blocks correctly: a wheel event dispatched directly over non-scrollable dialog chrome and over the chip grid both leave `window.scrollY` at `0`; `getComputedStyle(cardEl).touchAction` reads `"none"` at every sampled point across an open transition, with no inline `touchAction` override from Motion; and a CDP-synthetic touch-drag over the chip grid (iPhone 13 emulation) produces no native scroll at all. This matches this codebase's own established pattern for this exact bug class — the original fix's own writeup already notes real hardware's native touch pre-recognition happens at the compositor level, before JS or even `touch-action` computed-style inspection can fully observe it, and "doesn't always lose that race the same way [headless] tests... implied."

**Leading hypothesis, applied as a defensive fix per this codebase's own established practice for this bug class** (a targeted fix for the most likely cause, not a guess at a different one — see the real-device WebKit fixes above): `cardRef` only ever acquired an actual, non-identity CSS `transform` starting this session, once it picked up `layout="position"` (previously only `growableRef` — a plain, non-sticky descendant — ever carried one). `position: sticky` combined with an active `transform` *on the same element* is a documented category of WebKit quirk; their interaction with the compositor's own scroll/gesture recognition is inconsistent across iOS versions. Fixed by splitting the two concerns onto two different elements: a new, bare outer `<div>` carries `sticky`/`z-[var(--z-overlay)]`/`touch-none`/the real, instant `marginTop` — no border, padding, background, or Motion involvement of any kind. The `motion.div` (`cardRef`, unchanged in every other respect — same `layout="position"`, same `transition`) moves one level in, carrying only the visual border/padding/background/shadow and Motion's `transform`; it's never itself positioned, just an ordinary in-flow block filling its sticky parent's box. `cardRef` itself (the ref variable) now points at the inner element, unchanged everywhere else it's read (`.contains()` for click-outside, `.getBoundingClientRect()` for measurement) — a plain, non-positioned child's own rect still correctly reflects wherever the browser actually renders it regardless of which ancestor supplies the positioning, so none of those call sites needed to change. Verified: `getComputedStyle(outerWrapper).transform` reads `none` at every sampled point, including mid-transition — the two properties genuinely never coexist on one element anymore — and the full existing verification suite (drag, resize snaps, open/close, stress test, click-outside) still passes unchanged. **This is a defensive fix for the leading hypothesis, not a confirmed root-cause fix** — needs on-device confirmation before treating it as resolved.

### The sticky/transform split above wasn't the actual regression — it was `pinScrollToDocumentBottomIfWasAtBottom`'s own deferred timing, from earlier this same session

Reported live, on-device, after the split above shipped: still happening. Pointed directly at this exact bug's own established, working history (the `blockScrollThrough`/`touch-action`/`data-scroll-fade-orientation` entries above) rather than another guess — the right instinct: that whole mechanism was never touched this session and was independently re-verified still fully intact (wheel dispatch over both non-scrollable dialog chrome and the chip grid still leaves `scrollY` at `0`; `touch-action` still computes `none` throughout). If the original fix is still standing untouched and the bug is back, the regression has to be somewhere *else* nearby, not in that mechanism itself.

It was: earlier this same session, chasing the `cardRef`/Row 2 desync on a large-persisted-height reopen (a few entries up in this document), `pinScrollToDocumentBottomIfWasAtBottom`'s own editMode-toggle call site was changed from synchronous (`useLayoutEffect`, firing immediately as `editMode` flips true) to deferred (`useEffect` + `setTimeout(..., EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS)`), reasoning that `window.scrollTo` firing *during* Motion's own transform animation on that sticky element was what desynced it from Row 2. True as far as it went — but deferring the correction means the page sits at a stale, uncorrected scroll position for the *entire* ~280ms transition whenever `wasAtDocumentBottomRef` is true, which is exactly the shape of bug this file's own "real mechanism behind mashing an empty slot" entry (above) already documents as a trigger for genuine WebKit compositor desync: a sudden, forced, non-gesture scroll discontinuity is precisely what a compositor's incremental layer-position tracking can lose sync on, especially for a `position: sticky` element changing size in the same window. Traded a narrower bug (a rare reopen scenario, discovered through this session's own testing, never reported by the user) for a much more consequential regression of a bug the user already knew about and had already fixed once.

**Fix**: reverted `pinScrollToDocumentBottomIfWasAtBottom`'s toggle call site back to exactly its original shape — synchronous `useLayoutEffect`, firing immediately, default 6-frame repeat, no parameters. This necessarily reopens the large-persisted-height-reopen desync it was traded for (confirmed live: back to `393px` maxDelta on Row 2 for that one specific scenario — reopening directly at a big, persisted pool height, from a page scrolled to its exact true bottom) — an accepted, explicit trade-off, not a silent regression: a rare edge case this session's own testing found, versus a common one the user actually hit on real hardware. **The generalized tell, worth carrying forward: when two fixes in the same file are in genuine tension (each one's correction is the other one's regression trigger), the fix for a bug the user has actually reported and hit takes priority over a fix for a bug found only through this session's own testing — revert to the known-good behavior for the reported one, and document the reopened edge case explicitly rather than trying to thread both through a single, cleverer mechanism under time pressure.**

### The page and the panel were still scrolling together as one unit, on-device — `dialogRef`'s own `aria-modal="true"` was never actually backed by a background-scroll lock

Reported live, after the revert above shipped: the free scroll-through was gone (confirmed, on-device, that the revert helped), but a related, previously-indistinguishable symptom was still there once the bigger bug stopped masking it — with the edit panel open, the page and the panel visibly scrolled together as one unit, even though the panel's own size/position never changed. Explicit requirement, stated directly: scroll should be isolated to the panel's own internal scroll areas only; the outer page/window shouldn't scroll at all while the panel is open.

This is a different bug from the scroll-through entries above, not the same one recurring: `blockScrollThrough` (and `touch-action: none` on `cardRef`) only ever protected gestures *starting on the bar itself* from leaking to the page — that's still fully intact and doing its job. Nothing in this file ever stopped a gesture starting *elsewhere on the page* (the results section, say) from scrolling the document while the panel happened to be open, nor stopped the page's own native rubber-band/scroll-chaining once a nested `ScrollFadeArea` inside the panel bottoms out — WebKit's own `overscroll-behavior` support has a documented history of being less reliable at the true document boundary than between two merely-nested scroll containers, which the existing `overscroll-y-contain` on `html`/`body` (globals.css) already works around for the page's *own* boundary, but doesn't address a nested scroller chaining *up into* the page above it.

`dialogRef` already carries `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal="true"` — the semantics of a modal already imply the background shouldn't be scrollable while it's open, but nothing before this actually enforced that.

**Fix**: `html.mixbar-panel-scroll-locked` (globals.css), toggled by a plain `useEffect` keyed on `editMode` (`document.documentElement.classList.add/remove`, mirroring `blockScrollThrough`'s own identical `hide-scrollbar-while-blocked` toggle shape). `overflow: hidden` blocks wheel/programmatic document scroll outright, no JS needed for that half. `touch-action: none` closes the touch half, mirroring `cardRef`'s own identical pairing (see the very first scroll-through entry in this document) at the whole-document scope instead of just the bar's own box — compositor-level touch-pan recognition happens before JS runs, so `overflow: hidden` alone doesn't reliably stop native touch-driven bounce/scroll on its own, the same lesson that original fix already learned, just needing to be re-applied at a wider scope this time. Every `ScrollFadeArea` instance already carves its own axis back out via `touch-pan-x`/`touch-pan-y` directly on itself, regardless of how far up the tree a `touch-action: none` was declared (`touch-action`'s used value resolves from the element a touch actually starts on, not force-inherited from an ancestor) — so the panel's own chip grid and tab strip keep scrolling internally exactly as before, confirmed live: a wheel gesture directly over the chip grid still moves its own `scrollTop` (`0 → 14`, its real max at the tested viewport) while `window.scrollY` stays untouched at `0` for the same gesture. Verified the full toggle lifecycle: absent in browse mode, present the instant edit mode opens, removed the instant it closes, page scroll works normally again immediately after.

One side effect, not chased further since it wasn't asked for and matches the modal semantics already declared: any *other* `ScrollFadeArea` elsewhere on the page (the results carousel, say) that doesn't carry its own explicit touch-pan override would also lose page-level touch scroll while the panel is open — every instance in this app already does carry one, so nothing currently regresses, but it's the honest scope of what "whole document" locking actually means, worth remembering if a future `ScrollFadeArea` caller is ever added without its own explicit orientation.

### A parallel, on-device-only report — Row 2 "jittering"/re-rendering during a slow manual resize-drag — investigated exhaustively, nothing found

Reported alongside the scroll-through regression above, with two useful, specific details: it seems tied to *slow* dragging specifically, and — critically — it does **not** reproduce on desktop, nor on a mobile *viewport emulated* in a desktop browser pointed at `localhost`, only on the user's actual physical device. That last detail alone is a strong, direct signal: identical logical behavior (same JS, same values, same emulated viewport size), only a real device's own rendering/compositor pipeline differs — the same category as the scroll-through bug and this file's several other WebKit-real-device-only entries, not something a Mac-hosted headless or emulated browser can be expected to surface.

Tried anyway, exhaustively, since a concrete new detail ("slow" drag, "gap or padding inconsistency" between the panel and Row 2) is worth chasing before writing it off: a 60-step, 1px-per-step drag (the finest-grained simulation of "slow" this tooling can produce, well beyond the 10px-per-step sampling used elsewhere in this file), tracking not just Row 2's own position but the *gap* between the panel's own bottom edge and Row 2's top edge, `dialogHeight`, and `TagPoolBody`'s own live-measured `chipAreaHeightPx` (which reduces `poolPanelHeightPx` by its own `ResizeObserver`-measured tab-row height — a real, independent piece of state that could plausibly lag a frame behind MixerBar's own direct style updates, which is exactly the shape of bug the user's own "gap or padding inconsistency" hunch describes). Every one of the 60 sampled frames: the gap held at exactly `16px` (`DIALOG_MARGIN_BOTTOM_PX`, unchanged), `dialogHeight` increased by exactly `1px` per step with zero non-monotonic steps (never once decreased during an upward drag), and `chipAreaHeightPx` tracked it in exact lockstep throughout. Nothing here lags, races, or drifts, even at this granularity.

**Given the exhaustive audit two entries up already ruled out every measurable dimension in Row 2 itself, and this pass additionally rules out the panel/Row-2 boundary specifically, the leading explanation is the same one already on record for the scroll-through bug: a real device's own paint/composite pipeline, not anything `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` can see.** Worth trying, if it comes up again: this codebase has *already* successfully used the user's own phone connected via Safari's remote Web Inspector to catch a live layout/paint divergence directly (the element-highlight overlay visibly offset from the actually-painted box, in the "real mechanism behind mashing an empty slot" entry above) — the same technique (or Safari's own Timelines/Performance panel, watching for dropped frames specifically during a slow drag) is the most direct next step for this one, since it's the one approach in this file's own history that has actually confirmed a real-device compositor issue directly rather than by inference from a defensive fix's own before/after report.

### `AnimationDebugPanel.tsx` — a live-tweakable overlay for every Motion timing/easing value in the app, requested directly to help isolate which value was causing which symptom during the panel-animation debugging above

A small, collapsible, `fixed`-positioned panel (top-right, behind an "anim debug" toggle button so it doesn't sit permanently on top of `Masthead`'s own theme swatches in the same corner), rendered once at `KonomiApp.tsx`'s own root. Backed by `animationDebugStore.ts`, the same `useSyncExternalStore`-backed shared-mutable-store pattern already established elsewhere in this app (`tagPoolPrefs.ts`'s `usePoolPanelHeight`, `RecommendationsBody.tsx`'s empty-state shuffle-bag) — a plain module-level value plus a listener set, readable non-reactively (`getAnimationDebugValues()`, for plain event-handler closures like `rollBusyRef`'s `setTimeout` scheduling) as well as reactively (`useAnimationDebugValues()`, for render bodies). Every previously-hardcoded animation constant across the app now has a live-tweakable counterpart here: `MixerBar.tsx`'s `EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS`/ease (`layoutTransition`, driving `cardRef`/Row 2's `layout="position"` above), `RollingLabel.tsx`'s roll duration/ease (and its three callers' own `rollBusyRef` gates, which now read the live value directly rather than the static import, so the gate's own duration can never drift out of sync with a debug-panel-tweaked roll), and `cardAnimTuning.ts`'s card enter/exit duration/stagger/distance/both eases. `ANIMATION_DEBUG_DEFAULTS` mirrors every one of these values' pre-existing, already-shipped numbers exactly, so opening the panel and touching nothing reproduces today's real behavior bit-for-bit. Deliberately **not** persisted to `localStorage` — resets to defaults on every reload, matching the same "in-memory only" scoping this app's own prior `CardAnimDebugPanel` used (see the card-migration entry above) — a stray debug value should never accidentally look like production behavior after a session ends. This whole module (the store, the panel component, and every call site threaded through it) is a temporary tuning aid, not a permanent feature — remove it, and inline whatever values it settles on back into each file's own plain constant, once this app's remaining Motion feel/timing questions are resolved.

## Mixer error system, and why there's no live filtering at all

**`RecommendationsBody`'s placeholder box renders on `!hasResults`, not `empty` (`!generated`).** An error still sets `generated: true` (see `recommend()` below) while leaving `results` untouched — on a first-ever RECOMMEND attempt (or right after Clear ✕), `results` is already `[]`, so the moment an error fires, `empty` goes `false` (generated is now true) *and* `hasResults` stays `false` (still zero results) — a third state the original `empty`/`hasResults` two-way branch never accounted for. With the condition as `empty`, that state rendered neither the placeholder nor the results grid, collapsing the whole section down to just its header, then jumping back to full height the moment a real result set finally loaded — reported as "the placeholder disappears completely, we don't want a jarring height change." `!hasResults` covers both "never generated" and "generated but errored with nothing to show," so the placeholder (and the `--result-card-height` it reserves) stays put across an error the whole time. The now-redundant `empty` field was removed from `useKonomi`'s return value and the prop chain entirely, rather than left unused.

**The displayed result set is always exactly `state.results` — there is no filtered/derived view sitting in front of it.** An earlier version of this app had a `visibleResults` layer that hid stored entries live based on min-match (frozen `entry.score` vs a live threshold). That's been removed entirely: it turned out to be the wrong model. The actual requirement is simpler and stricter — **only RECOMMEND and Clear ✕ change what's visible.** Adjusting MIN TAG MATCH or changing/swapping/removing tags never hides or shows a card; they only change what *setting* the next RECOMMEND will use, and (for tags) what each already-visible card's live score badge and highlights show. Remove every tag down to zero and every card stays put, honestly showing `0/0` — nothing to hide, because hiding isn't a thing tag or min-match changes do anymore.

This means `entry.score` (the field stored on each `ResultEntry`) is now purely informational — nothing reads it to decide whether a card is shown. The only score that ever drives anything a user sees is the *live* `scoreSeries(series, selectedTags)` call inside `ResultCard`, used for the count badge and tag highlighting, recomputed fresh on every render regardless of what's stored. Because there's no visibility filter left to satisfy, the locked-carry-forward path in `recommend()` no longer needs to clamp its score to the current min-match either (see below) — it's just the honest live score, kept only for whatever informational value it has.

**`state.error` — reducer state, set only by RECOMMEND, cleared only by specific actions.** This can't be a derived value — if it were computed live from `tagCount < minMatch`, it would flicker on/off automatically as tags or min-match change, which directly contradicts "errors fire only on RECOMMEND." So it's real state (`MixerError = "impossible-min" | "no-match" | null` in `types.ts`), mutated by exactly one reducer function (`recommend()`, which sets it) and a handful of specific clearing rules elsewhere. On either error, `results` is deliberately left completely untouched.

**Priority order inside `recommend()`, checked in this sequence every time:** first `sel.length < min` → `"impossible-min"` (never touches the catalog at all); else run the real `scorePool` rescore, and if it comes back empty → `"no-match"` — checked as `pool.length === 0`, not `out.length === 0`. This distinction matters: locked cards surviving in `out` doesn't mean the error shouldn't fire, since the error is about whether a *fresh* search for *new* matches succeeded, which is an entirely separate question from whether anything old is still on screen. A `RECOMMEND` can legitimately show "no series match this mix" right next to two locked cards that are still sitting there from before.

**Clearing tracks current state, not which action originally triggered the error.** `"no-match"` clears on *any* tag or min-match change — the whole combination was the problem, so any edit is a fresh attempt worth trying again. `"impossible-min"` clears whenever the underlying condition (`tagCount < min`) stops being true, from either side: `SET_MIN_MATCH` lowering the threshold to `<= tagCount`, *or* `clearErrorForTagChange` seeing `tagCount` rise back to `>= min` (a manual tag add, or Random ×3 refilling all slots) — both are handled, not just the min-match-control path. The first version of this only cleared on the min-match side, which left a real gap: erroring at min-3 with 2 tags, then adding a third tag, should obviously clear it (2 tags making min-3 impossible has stopped being true), but nothing re-evaluated the error until the user touched MIN TAG MATCH itself. `clearErrorForTagChange` also still handles the separate global-override case — tag count dropping to exactly zero clears either error type, since `min-match` can never be lower than `"1"`, so `tagCount >= min` is never satisfied by zero tags on its own; the zero-tags case needs its own explicit check. Called from every slot-mutating action (`TOGGLE_TAG`, `CLEAR_SLOT`, `PLACE_IN_SLOT`, `CLEAR_SLOTS`, `PRUNE_TAGS`, `RANDOM_TAGS`).

**`SET_MIN_MATCH` no longer calls `recommend()`.** It used to cascade a full rescore whenever results were already showing. That's now handled entirely by live filtering (which needs no reducer action at all) — the reducer's job on a min-match change is just to update the value and apply the two clearing rules above, nothing else.

**Visual treatment reuses the existing disabled styling, but decouples two conditions that used to be one.** `RecommendButton`'s star has always dimmed to `opacity-35` when disabled (unchanged, still tied to `!enabled`, now `enabled = filledCount > 0 && !error`). What's new: the star's *rotation* is tied to a different condition (`!error`, not `!enabled`) — so the pre-existing zero-tags state still dims while continuing to spin (unchanged behavior), but an active error additionally freezes it, giving the two disabled states a distinct feel even though they share a color treatment.

**Freezing uses `animation-play-state`, not adding/removing the animation class.** An earlier version toggled the whole `animate-[kono-spin_…]` class off on error, which stops the animation by dropping its `transform` entirely — the star snapped back to its base 0deg orientation on every error, then jumped again on resume, instead of pausing wherever it happened to be mid-spin. The animation class now stays on permanently; only `[animation-play-state:paused]` / `[animation-play-state:running]` toggles with `error`. Pausing a CSS animation freezes its current computed transform in place (the browser already tracks elapsed animation time — no manual angle capture needed), and un-pausing resumes from that exact point rather than restarting. Verified live via `getComputedStyle(el).transform`: the paused matrix stays byte-identical across an 800ms wait, and the first frame after resuming shows a small delta from the paused matrix rather than a jump back to the identity matrix.

**Star size (150px outer / 139px inner, previously 190px / 176px) is a plain visual-weight reduction** — the two layers keep the same ~0.93 size ratio to each other, just scaled down together.

**The upward-shift layout is a precise formula, not a guessed offset.** The star+button assembly sits in a `min-h-[92px] flex items-center justify-center` box; adding a taller subtext below and letting the box grow would leave the button pinned at the top (`items-start` in the row above already fixes that edge), not shifted up. Instead, `RecommendButton` applies a `translate-y` to the star+button wrapper (Tailwind v4 note: individual translate utilities compile to the native CSS `translate` property now, not `transform` — check `getComputedStyle(el).translate`, not `.transform`, when verifying) and renders the subtext `position: absolute, top-full`, matching the same out-of-flow hint pattern used for `LevelSelector`/`TagPool`'s hints elsewhere in this file. The shift amount is `(gap + subtextLineHeight) / 2` — half of what's being added below — which is exactly the offset that makes the *new* block (button + gap + subtext) center on the same point the button *alone* used to occupy in that fixed 92px box. Measured live: `9.5px` (`4px` gap + `15px` line height), verified to a `0px` diff between the box's natural center and the shifted block's center.

**The old "no series match that strict" banner in `RecommendationsBody` is retired.** It's now redundant with the button-adjacent error message. `hasResults`/`empty` keep their prior meaning (`generated && results.length > 0` / `!generated`); there's no longer a distinct banner for "generated, but currently nothing to show" — that state (which can now only happen right after an errored `RECOMMEND`, since a *successful* one always produces at least one result) is deliberately blank in the grid area, since the error message at the button already explains it, and duplicating that explanation with slightly different copy would be more confusing than nothing.

### Locked cards are real, positioned members of `results` — not a visual overlay

**`recommend()` walks the raw stored order directly, and that's correct — not a simplification that cuts a corner.** An earlier version of this logic walked a separately-filtered "current visible" list instead of `state.results`, because at the time a card could be hidden live (by min-match) while still being stored — meaning stored order and displayed order could diverge, and a locked card could visually "jump" position across a RECOMMEND if the loop walked the wrong one. That entire divergence is gone now that there's no live filtering at all (see the section above): the displayed set *is* `state.results`, always, so there is no separate "visible order" left to reconcile against. Walking the raw array directly is therefore the *correct* model again, not a reversion that reintroduces a bug — the bug it used to cause can't occur without the live-filtering layer that originally created the mismatch.

**Relative order + compaction still falls out of the loop shape for free, unrelated to any of that.** `recommend()` walks `state.results[i]` for `i` in `0..DEFAULT_RESULT_COUNT`, pushing (a) the locked entry when found at that index or (b) the next fresh pool pick when one's available, skipping only when neither applies. Locked entries get visited and pushed in the same relative sequence they already had (order preserved for free), and a skipped iteration contributes nothing rather than a gap (compaction for free) — this was true before the live-filtering detour and remains true now that it's been removed.

**Locked-carry-forward no longer clamps its score.** It used to compute `Math.max(scoreSeries(old.series, sel), min)` specifically to guarantee a carried-over locked entry would satisfy the live visibility filter's `score >= min` check even after being unlocked. With no visibility filter left to satisfy, that clamp was solving a problem that no longer exists — it's now a plain `scoreSeries(old.series, sel)`, the honest live score, kept only because `ResultEntry.score` is still a field on the type (informational, read by nothing).

**`REPLACE_CARD` still targets by title, not index — this didn't need to change.** It moved to title-based targeting during the live-filtering era for a reason that no longer applies (displayed and stored order could diverge), but title is the same stable identifier `TOGGLE_LOCK`/`TOGGLE_FLIP` already use, and there's no reason to revert something that's strictly more robust just because its original motivating scenario went away.

## Result-card enter/exit animation

RECOMMEND and Clear ✕ used to swap `results` instantly. Now: RECOMMEND rises+fades new cards in, left-to-right, ease-out; Clear ✕ sinks+fades the removed cards out, right-to-left, ease-in (a deliberate bookend — an earlier version had exit also rise, tried live, then changed to sink on request). Five named constants in `cardAnimTuning.ts`, same convention as `ROLL_DURATION_MS` (`RollingLabel.tsx`)/`EDIT_PANEL_TRANSITION_MS` (`MixerBar.tsx`) — `CARD_ANIM_DURATION_MS`/`CARD_ANIM_STAGGER_MS`/`CARD_ANIM_DISTANCE_PX`/`CARD_ANIM_EASE_IN`/`CARD_ANIM_EASE_OUT` (current values: 300/50/8/`cubic-bezier(0,0,.2,1)`/`cubic-bezier(0,0,.2,1)`, both easing curves the same by request). These were dialed in live via a temporary debug panel, since removed for production — see that section below for the full story.

### Which cards animate in: a batch-scoped `newTitles` field, not `firstNewIndex` and not `!locked` — three designs tried, in this order, each rejected for a different concrete reason

1. **`index >= firstNewIndex`** — rejected by tracing `recommend()`'s loop (`useKonomi.ts`) closely: it walks index `0..DEFAULT_RESULT_COUNT-1`, and at *each* index independently decides "carry the locked entry" or "take the next pool pick" — locked entries can land at any index, not just a prefix. A user locking card #3 while leaving #1/#2/#4/#5 unlocked, then hitting RECOMMEND, produces `[fresh, fresh, locked, fresh, fresh]`; `firstNewIndex` reads `0` (correct for the carousel's own scroll-reset, which only needs "the first fresh one"), but index 2 isn't fresh — `index >= firstNewIndex` would incorrectly replay the entrance on that untouched, non-remounted locked card.
2. **`isEntering = !locked[entry.series.title]`**, tried next as a simplification once `newTitles` (below) had first solved the interleaving problem — reasoned to be equivalent in practice, since every non-locked slot in `recommend()`'s output is always a fresh pick. Rejected once confirmed live: unlike `newTitles`, this derivation has no batch-scoping at all, so it's a function of the *live, currently-mutable* `locked` map — locking a card and then unlocking it again, with no RECOMMEND in between, makes `isEntering` genuinely toggle `false → true`, and confirmed live (via `element.getAnimations()`, checking `playState`/`currentTime`, not just the class list) that this really does restart the CSS animation rather than just holding its already-finished end state. Requested directly to be fixed: locking/unlocking must leave an on-screen card completely stable.
3. **Final: `newTitles: string[]` on `KonomiState`**, recomputed inside `recommend()`'s existing loop (exactly the titles pulled from `pool`, i.e., genuine fresh picks *this call*) and reset to `[]` in `clearRecs()` (both branches) for hygiene, though nothing depends on that reset for correctness — locked entries are naturally excluded from `newTitles` at the source (they hit the "carry old" branch, never the "push a fresh pick" one), so there's no need to separately re-derive or reset anything to keep them out. `ResultCard`'s `isEntering` prop is `newTitles.includes(entry.series.title)`. This is what actually satisfies the "lock/unlock must not touch a stable card" requirement: `newTitles` only ever changes inside `recommend()`, and locking/unlocking a card only ever touches `KonomiState.locked` — two completely disjoint pieces of state, so a lock toggle can never flip `isEntering` for any card, confirmed live the same way (`getAnimations()` stays `finished`/unchanged through a lock-then-unlock sequence with no RECOMMEND in between).

One accepted side effect of `newTitles`, unchanged from the `!locked` version: a single-card dice swap (`REPLACE_CARD`) doesn't touch `newTitles` at all (only `recommend()` does), so a swapped-in card does *not* play the enter animation — this reverted back to the original, narrower scope (RECOMMEND-only) once `newTitles` came back, rather than the incidentally-broader "any unlocked card" scope the `!locked` version had introduced.

### `isExiting` must be checked before `isEntering` when picking the animation class/easing — confirmed live as a real bug, twice, under two different `isEntering` implementations

Both the `newTitles`-based and the final `!locked`-based `isEntering` share the same failure mode if `ResultCard`'s class-selection ternary checks `isEntering` first: on Clear ✕, a card that's about to exit is *also* still "entering" by either definition (its title was never removed from `newTitles`, or it was simply never locked) — so `isEntering ? enter-class : isExiting ? exit-class : ...` picks the wrong keyframe (and wrong easing) for exactly the cards that should be exiting. Caught live both times via a Playwright script reading the wrapper's actual `classList`/computed `animationName` right after a lock-then-RECOMMEND-then-Clear sequence — the delay math alone looked correct even with the bug present (`RecommendationsBody` computes delay independently of which class ends up applied), so inspecting delay values alone wouldn't have caught it. Fixed permanently by ordering the ternary `isExiting ? exit-class : isEntering ? enter-class : ...` (`ResultCard.tsx`) — `isExiting` is the one genuinely time-boxed, unambiguous signal here (only true during an actual in-flight exit), so it has to win the overlap regardless of how `isEntering` ends up being computed upstream.

### `clearGen` — an explicit "Clear ✕ just fired" signal, not inferred from diffing `results`

`clearRecs()` is a synchronous reducer action: it removes non-locked entries from `results` in the same tick the button is clicked, with no window for an exit animation to play before the DOM nodes would otherwise vanish. Since `useKonomi.ts` is deliberately DOM/animation-free (see "React Native portability" below), the deferral has to live in the component layer — but the component needs one unambiguous signal for "a clear just happened," not something inferred by diffing `results` (fragile: a single-card `REPLACE_CARD` swap also changes that reference, and could be misread as a partial clear). `KonomiState` gained `clearGen: number`, bumped once per `CLEAR_RECS` dispatch, mirroring `recommendGen`'s existing pattern exactly.

`RecommendationsBody.tsx` owns a `displayedResults` state that lags one step behind the real `results` prop specifically across a clear: on a `clearGen` change, it computes which titles disappeared (diffing the old `displayedResults` against the new `results`) and, if any did, marks them `exitingTitles` *without* yet syncing `displayedResults` to the shorter array — so the exiting cards stay mounted and keep playing their exit keyframe. A `useEffect` keyed on `exitingTitles` (only fires on the empty→non-empty transition) waits `durationMs + (lastExitIndex) * staggerMs` via `setTimeout` — not `animationend`, this app has confirmed animation-completion *events* unreliable enough on real devices to avoid entirely (`RollingLabel.tsx`/`MixerBar.tsx`'s own history) — then finally syncs `displayedResults` to the real `results` and clears `exitingTitles`. `showResultsGrid` (`hasResults || exitingTitles.size > 0`) keeps the results grid (and the empty-state placeholder gated off) through that whole window too — `hasResults` alone flips false the instant `clearRecs()` dispatches, which would otherwise show the empty-state copy jarringly early, mid-exit-animation.

Per-card exit stagger uses each card's own index within the *full* displayed row, not a compacted exiting-only count — `(displayedResults.length - 1 - index) * effectiveStaggerMs` — so a locked card that stays put doesn't shift the timing of the ones sweeping around it; it just sits still while the sweep passes its slot.

### Stagger scales down proportionally — paced by the breakpoint tier's visible card count, minus whichever of those are locked, not by total result count

`CARD_ANIM_STAGGER_MS` (`cardAnimTuning.ts`) is defined as the value at `DEFAULT_RESULT_COUNT` (5) cards. `getEffectiveStaggerMs(staggerMs, count)` (`cardAnimTuning.ts`) scales it proportionally — `staggerMs * count / DEFAULT_RESULT_COUNT`.

The first version fed `displayedResults.length` (the total result count) into that `count`. Reported live as wrong: the stagger is perceived relative to how many cards visibly sweep together in the current viewport, not the total, and a narrow carousel tier only ever shows a couple of cards at once (with a peek of the next) regardless of how many total results exist — the full-count pace read as too slow/spread out there.

**Fixed by tying `count` to the same responsive breakpoint tiers `ResultCard.tsx`'s own card-width classes already use** — `getVisibleCardTierCount(width)` (`cardAnimTuning.ts`) mirrors those exact breakpoints (`1152/993/741/501px` → `5/4/3/2/1` cards), returned as a plain number, not shared code — Tailwind's arbitrary-value classes need literal static text for its scanner, so there's no single source of truth the CSS and this JS mirror could actually share directly (same constraint as `IconButton.tsx`'s `BORDER_CLASSES`). `RecommendationsBody.tsx`'s own `useVisibleCardTierCount()` hook tracks this live via a `resize` listener (width genuinely changes at runtime far more than, say, `ResultCard.tsx`'s `useSupportsHover`'s hover-capability check does, so unlike that hook this one can't get away with a one-time read).

**Locked cards are then excluded from that tier count** — `getAnimatingPaceCount(titles, locked, visibleTierCount)` takes the first `visibleTierCount` titles (by row position) and counts how many are *not* locked, since a locked card never animates and shouldn't count toward "how many are visibly sweeping." This deliberately reads the *live* `locked` map, not `newTitles` — a different question from "does this card play the animation at all" (which must stay decoupled from live lock state, see the section above): pace is a cosmetic "how tight should the sweep read right now" question, so reacting live to lock changes is correct and intended here specifically, not a contradiction with `isEntering`'s own batch-scoping.

This combined pace count is computed once per render/per exit-effect-firing and fed into `getEffectiveStaggerMs` for **both** the per-card delay math and the exit-timeout effect's total-duration calculation — both need to agree on the same number, or the timer would fire before/after the visual sweep actually finishes. Per-card delays still use each card's own *index within the full displayed row* (not a compacted entering/exiting-only index) — a locked card sitting still doesn't shift the timing of the ones sweeping around it.

### A single-card dice swap now gets the same rise+fade-in entrance, at 0 delay, plus a real bug fixed along the way

The dice/swap icon (`REPLACE_CARD`) never touched `newTitles`, so a swapped-in card never played the enter animation — deliberately, at first (RECOMMEND-only was the original scope). Requested directly to extend it: a swap should rise+fade in exactly like a RECOMMEND entrance (same `CARD_ANIM_DURATION_MS`/easing/`CARD_ANIM_DISTANCE_PX`), just as the *only* card entering right now, not part of a batch.

`RecommendationsBody.tsx` already tracked `justSwappedIndex` (which grid position just had its card swapped) for an unrelated reason — seeding `ResultCard`'s `startWithSwapHint`, so the brand-new instance that mounts there shows its "swap" hover/tap hint immediately rather than requiring a fresh hover. That same signal now also feeds `isEntering`: `isEntering = newTitles.includes(entry.series.title) || justSwappedIndex === index`. Its delay is forced to `0` rather than `index * effectiveStaggerMs` — a swap is always exactly one card regardless of its row position, so there's no stagger to wait through; the same "single card, no one to sweep with" pacing `getAnimatingPaceCount` already produces for a RECOMMEND that happens to leave only one card entering. No exit animation on the replaced card — a swap never touches `clearGen`, so the exit-orchestration machinery (`exitingTitles`/`displayedResults`) never engages for it; the old card is simply gone the instant `results` updates, same as before this change, and the new one rises in on top of that same-instant swap.

**A real, previously-unrelated bug surfaced and was fixed in the same pass**: `justSwappedIndex` was never reset after being set, so swapping a card and then hitting RECOMMEND right after left it stale — the fresh card that mounts at that same grid position (a RECOMMEND replaces most/all unlocked cards, remember) also satisfied `justSwappedIndex === index`, seeding *its* `hoveredIcon` to `"swap"` even though nothing was actually swapped this time. Reported live: the swap tooltip showed immediately on the new card and stayed stuck until tapping elsewhere, on both desktop and mobile — consistent with `startWithSwapHint`'s own lazy initializer only running once, on that instance's first render. Fixed by resetting `justSwappedIndex` to `null` the instant `recommendGen` changes (the render-phase conditional `setState` pattern, comparing against a tracked previous value in state rather than a ref — this session's established way to satisfy `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`/`react-hooks/refs` simultaneously). Confirmed live: a swap's own tooltip still shows correctly right after a real swap, and no longer reappears on the next RECOMMEND's fresh card at that position.

### Where the transform lands: one level inside the carousel's snap/gesture root, never on it

The enter/exit `translateY`/opacity animation applies to the perspective wrapper div (`<div className="relative w-full h-full [perspective:1100px]">`, `ResultCard.tsx`), never to the outer `<article>`. That `<article>` is the carousel's actual snap/gesture-participating box, and this file has extensive, already-confirmed-live history of keeping it transform-free — the 3D `perspective` itself was deliberately moved off it earlier, specifically because a transform/perspective on the snap-item broke native touch-scroll-gesture-chaining on real iOS. The wrapper one level in is already established as safe to carry visual transforms (the flip's own `rotateY` lives one level further in still), and is structurally separate from the snap/gesture root.

`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is handled in two places, not just CSS: `globals.css` forces `animation-duration: 1ms`/`transform: none` on both keyframe classes (so there's no visible rise even under the "both" fill-mode), and `RecommendationsBody`'s own exit-timeout effect separately checks the same media query and uses a `0ms` timeout in that case — confirmed live this matters: without the JS-side check, `displayedResults` would still wait the *full* (non-reduced) duration to sync even though the CSS motion itself was already near-instant, leaving faded-but-still-mounted cards sitting invisible for the remainder.

### The live-tweak debug menu was temporary, and has now been removed for production
